### 2026-10-11: Step 1: c) "caught" when an alert is on ahead of the dip (method and prediction, written before computing)
- **What had been seen before this change** (by me and in the run): the provisional-rule results of the first development backtest (52 shortfall episodes, 26 caught, recall 50 percent, precision 77 percent, 0.041 false alerts per 30 days); the threshold curve on calibrated chances (recall rising with the threshold, 95 percent chosen); and the cause of that rise: of the dips caught at 95 percent and missed at 5 percent, all 15 are at DRENT-03, TIGHT-02 and TIGHT-03, where one alert episode stayed open across several dips, so no new episode opened in the 30 days before the later dips. **The change is motivated by that cause, not by a target number.** The real account has been seen too, so its numbers stay development.
- **The new definition.** A shortfall episode with first day D is caught if an alert was **on** at some ordinary origin O with O < D and D - O <= 30. "On" means an alert episode that opened at an ordinary origin and is open at O: opened on or before O and not closed on or before O (the look that closes an episode said "no alert", so the closing day is not "on"). An origin already in shortfall cannot be O (it is not ahead of time), and an alert episode that opened at an origin already in shortfall never counts, even while it stays open later (my reading of the plan's test "an episode that opened inside a shortfall does not count"; the other reading is still open). Lead time = D minus the earliest such O in the 30 days. The false-alert definition, precision, false alerts per 30 days, alert episodes and shortfall episodes (with the 3-day merge) are unchanged.
- **Applied everywhere at once:** the provisional rule, every threshold and every arm of step 3. Every report prints both: "opened ahead (original)" and "on ahead (new)".
- **The threshold is chosen again on raw chances** (calibration is shelved), with the new definition, on the 20 development personas of the 12-month set, from the records of a fresh development backtest of the current forecaster (500 futures, seed 42, weekday method; it includes the holiday fix, so its numbers differ slightly from the first backtest's at SAL-01 and TIGHT-01). Objective unchanged: the highest recall with precision at least 70 percent and at most 0.1 false alerts per 30 days, ordinary origins; ties: higher precision, then the higher threshold; thresholds 2, 5, 10, ..., 95 percent (probability-only rule); leave one persona out as before. The provisional rule (above 20 percent, or the median lowest balance below the buffer) is printed as its own row. Command: `python -m eval.thresholds_raw`, output `docs/results/thresholds_raw_2026-10-11.md` (the calibrated results are not overwritten). The 24-month set is not used to choose; step 3 applies the chosen threshold to it. Nothing changes in the app; the held-out personas get the definition and threshold fixed by then, in one run at the very end.
- **Prediction, written before computing:** under the new definition recall rises at low thresholds, mostly at the personas that hover near the buffer (DRENT-03, TIGHT-02, TIGHT-03), and moves little at high thresholds; precision, false alerts per 30 days, the number of alert episodes and the number of shortfall episodes do not change at all.
- **Stop line c)** (report the table and a diagnosis, choose no threshold; step 3 then reports the original definition only) if any of: recall under the new definition is lower than under the old at any threshold (a bug: the new caught set always contains the old one); precision, false alerts per 30 days, alert episodes or shortfall episodes differ between the two definitions; recall at thresholds of 80 percent and above rises by more than 5 percentage points; the leave-one-out folds choose more than three different thresholds across the 20 folds.

### 2026-10-11: Step 1 result: c) passes its checks; on raw chances the objective picks 90 percent (development, nothing changes in the app)
Fresh development backtest of the current forecaster (12-month set: 20 development personas, 800 origins of which 726 ordinary, 500 futures, seed 42, weekday method); 52 shortfall episodes. Full curve in `docs/results/thresholds_raw_2026-10-11.md` (`python -m eval.thresholds_raw`).

| Rule | Recall, opened ahead (original) | Recall, on ahead (new) | Precision | False alerts per 30 days |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5 percent | 50% (26/52) | 58% (30/52) | 49% (22/45) | 0.136 |
| 20 percent | 50% (26/52) | 58% (30/52) | 71% (22/31) | 0.053 |
| 50 percent | 46% (24/52) | 52% (27/52) | 81% (21/26) | 0.030 |
| 90 percent (chosen) | 60% (31/52) | 62% (32/52) | 96% (24/25) | 0.006 |
| 95 percent | 56% (29/52) | 56% (29/52) | 96% (22/23) | 0.006 |
| provisional rule (20 percent or median low under the buffer) | 50% (26/52) | 58% (30/52) | 71% (22/31) | 0.053 |

- **Stop conditions: none holds.** The new caught set contains the old one at every threshold; precision, false alerts, alert episodes and shortfall episodes are the same quantities for both definitions; at 80 percent and above recall rises by 0 to 2 points (at most 5 allowed); the leave-one-out folds chose 2 thresholds (65 and 90 percent; at most 3 allowed).
- **Prediction against result.** Recall rises at low thresholds (+8 points from 2 to 20 percent), as predicted, but at two of the three hovering personas only: DRENT-03 (+3) and TIGHT-03 (+1); TIGHT-02 gains none. At high thresholds it moves 0 to 2 points, as predicted. The rise is much smaller than the 15 dips that motivated the change, because under my reading an alert episode that opened while the account was already below the buffer never counts, even when it is still on at a later ordinary origin. Under the other reading (count it once it is on at an ordinary origin), 18 more dips would count at 5 percent and 9 more at 90 percent (TIGHT-03, DRENT-03, TIGHT-02, ITIGHT-03). That count was computed after seeing the table, for my own question only; it is not part of the method and nothing was chosen from it.
- **Threshold, development result:** on all 20 personas the objective picks **90 percent**: recall (new) 62 percent (32 of 52), precision 96 percent (24 of 25), 0.006 false alerts per 30 days, lead time 15.2 days (median 14). Leave one persona out (65 or 90 percent chosen on the other 19): recall (new) 56 percent (29 of 52), precision 91 percent (21 of 23), 0.012 false alerts per 30 days.
- The provisional rule's precision is 71 percent here, against 77 percent in the first backtest (2 more false alerts, 9 against 7): this backtest includes the holiday fix. The step 3 sanity check looks at which personas changed.
- Nothing changes in the app. The held-out run, when it comes, uses the definition and threshold fixed by then.

### 2026-10-11: Step 2: proposals for a) lump income and b) repeated large outflows (method, answer key, gates and prediction, written before the code)
**Common.** Everything at an origin t uses what the forecaster reads at t: rows posted on or before t, the recurring series as of t, flow types with my decisions as of t. A proposal or placement carries its evidence (the fingerprints of its rows) and is labelled "detected, not confirmed" until confirmed. Decisions are dated rows of a new table `proposal_decisions` (account, proposal key, action "looks right" or "not this time", decided on), replayed as of t like the recurring decisions: a decision dated after t is ignored. No page or button is built (out of scope); the forecaster reads the log, and `ForecastOptions` turns each part on (all off by default, so the app is unchanged).

**a) Lump income as proposals.**
- Lumps: `lump_rows` as on this branch (a big deposit in no series, not a pass-through, in the last 365 days).
- Sender: the sender text of the deposit (the rule of `eval/lump_senders.py`: the text without the bank's fixed words and slots), as the pseudonymizer's keyed PERSON token, never the name. A lump with no sender text gets no proposal and stays in the random arrivals (step 0). Without a pseudonym key there are no proposals (as for e-Transfer recipients); the synthetic evaluation uses a fixed made-up key. Two names that are one person are not merged (a limitation).
- A proposal needs at least 2 lumps from one sender. Typical size: the median size. Typical gap: the median of the gaps between consecutive lumps. Expected date: last lump plus the typical gap, moved to the business day before if it is not one (the way income is shifted). Window: expected date plus or minus the larger of 10 days and a quarter of the gap (rounded). A proposal exists only when the expected date is inside the 30-day horizon (an overdue one is not proposed: placing money is the optimistic side). 2 lumps: "possible"; 3 or more: "likely". Key: sender token and the date of the last lump, so a new lump makes a new proposal.
- Looks right: the deposit is placed at the typical size, on a day drawn uniformly inside the window in each future (its own random stream; days before the first forecast day move to it, days after the horizon fall outside it), on the expected date in the single-path estimate; the sender's lumps leave the random-arrival pool (rate and sizes are recomputed without them). It counts like an income series (it stays in the median without irregular deposits and in "no other money"). Not this time ("Not this month" on a page): nothing from that sender is placed, and the sender's lumps leave the pool too. Unconfirmed: the random arrivals of step 0, with every lump in the pool.
- Answer key (the generator's schedule objects, never the detector's thresholds). The planted income series are those whose id ends in -tuition-funding, -tuition-topup, -allowance, -aid or -family. A proposal's planted series are the planted income series of its evidence rows. A proposal is **right** if, for one of them, the first row posted after the origin falls inside the window. A planted series is **findable** at an origin if at least 2 of its rows are lumps at that origin; it counts for recall at that origin if its next row is posted inside the 30-day horizon.
- Scores, development personas, each set on its own (12-month and 24-month): precision (right / proposals, over origin-proposal pairs), recall (findable series with a right proposal at that origin / findable series, over origin-series pairs), date error (days between the expected date and the real next row, right proposals), size error (percent of the real size, right proposals), proposals by number of lumps behind them (2, 3, 4 or more) with their precision, false proposals per persona-year (distinct false proposals over persona-years, each origin standing for a week), and how often a lump had no sender text (origin-lump pairs). By archetype.
- **Gate (the plan's proposal):** the a) arms (2, 3, 5) run only if precision is at least 70 percent and recall at least 50 percent on at least one set. Otherwise the table and the reasons are written up and line a) stops.
- **Prediction:** precision is high where a sender sends one kind of deposit and falls where one sender sends both small regular transfers and big ones; recall is low on the 12-month set (few repeats) and better on the long set.

**b) Repeated large outflows, placed by default.**
- Big outflow: a row of the everyday-spending stream before the big-row rule (in no series, not a fee, not a netted hold; card purchases, cash, transfers and bill payments) posted in the 730 days up to t, at least the big-row cutoff at t (at least $500 and 5 times the typical outflow of the 13 weeks before t, or of everything when there are fewer than 5 outflows).
- Payee: the resolved merchant group (bill payments name the payee); for an outgoing e-Transfer the keyed token of the recipient (none printed: no payee); transfers to my own accounts are one payee; anything else has no payee and is never grouped.
- Detection: at least 2 big payments to one payee with at least one pair of consecutive payments 60 to 400 days apart (a gap under 60 days, such as a second installment 30 to 45 days later, is not a repeat; I check that it is excluded). Next date, the default rule: of (A) the same calendar day one year after the latest payment and (B) the latest payment plus the median of the 60-to-400-day gaps, the earliest that is after t and inside the horizon, moved to the next business day; none inside the horizon, no placement. A alone and B alone are also scored on the long set and reported beside the default. Amount: the latest payment (tuition rises, so the latest, not the median).
- Placement, by default, without a click, labelled "detected, not confirmed": on its date in the single-path estimate; in each future on a day drawn uniformly within 5 days either side (the plan's proposal; the generator draws tuition dates within 5 days), never before the first forecast day. Looks right: "confirmed", the date stays put. Not this time: that occurrence is skipped. Key: payee and the date of the latest payment. **This reverses the 2026-10-02 choice "no semester cadence; two irregular payees are not a pattern".**
- First time: one big payment to a payee and no other in the 730 days: no placement; a proposal object for a planned item prefilled from it (payee, amount, the same day one year on) is offered. Counted, not placed. The page is not built.
- Pass-through pairing is not changed. A placed tuition payment without its funding deposit makes the forecast pessimistic if the deposit has not arrived; a) placing a deposit without tuition makes it optimistic. Step 3 reports a) and b) alone and together.
- Answer key: the tuition payments of the generator (series id ending in -tuition). A placement is **right** if a tuition payment to the same payee is posted within 10 days of the placed date and its amount is within 20 percent of the placed amount. Precision: right / placements (origin-placement pairs). Recall: tuition payments posted inside an origin's horizon, among origins where that payee had at least 2 big payments by the origin, that a right placement matches (origin-payment pairs); also given over all origin-payment pairs, with the first-time cases (no placement possible) counted. Date error of right placements. By archetype. Scored on the long set; the 12-month set reported apart (expected weak).
- **Gate:** the b) arms (4, 5) run only if, on the long set, recall of placements is at least 50 percent among the origins where the payee had at least 2 payments (the plan's proposal) **and precision of placements is at least 70 percent (my addition: a wrong placement made by default is a false alarm).** Otherwise written up and line b) stops.
- **Prediction:** recall is high for the two-installment and summer-term personas (a clear gap) and for any persona with a second year of tuition, and zero for first-time cases by design; precision falls where installments 30 to 45 days apart look like a second payment (the 60-day minimum should exclude them).

**Tests for both:** rule zero (deleting or rewriting every later row leaves proposals and placements identical; a decision dated after t is ignored and one dated on t is used), a mutation check, determinism with a fixed seed, no name in any key (tokens only), "not this time" behaves as defined, one deposit or payment is never counted twice (a confirmed lump and its pool).

### 2026-10-11: Step 2 result: a) passes its gate on the 12-month set; b) fails its gate and stops
Scored with `python -m eval.proposals_score` (full tables in `docs/results/proposals_scores_2026-10-11.md`), 20 development personas per set, 40 weekly origins each. Counts are of (origin, proposal), (origin, series) or (origin, payment) pairs; origins within a persona overlap, so the persona and series counts are the honest n.

**a) Lump proposals.**

| Set | Proposals | Precision | Findable (origin, series) | Recall | Date error, median | Size error, median | False proposals per persona-year |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 12-month | 49 | 82% (40/49) | 63 (7 series, 7 personas) | 56% (35/63) | 3 days | 39% | 0.2 |
| 24-month | 81 | 69% (56/81) | 118 (9 series, 7 personas) | 38% (45/118) | 8 days | 29% | 0.7 |

- **Gate: pass** (precision at least 70 percent and recall at least 50 percent on the 12-month set). The a) arms (2 and 3) run in step 3.
- Which archetypes: almost all of it is ITIGHT (12-month: 42 of 49 proposals, 33 right, recall 31 of 56 pairs; 24-month: 56 of 81, 39 right) and IFAM (12-month 6 of 6 right, recall 3 of 6; 24-month 9 of 13 right, recall 6 of 20). DHOME-02's family contributions (24-month: 8 of 8 right) and one DRENT aid sender (12-month: 1 of 1) add a little. SAL, SEM, TIGHT and FREE get no proposals (FREE's deposits have no sender name: 4 of 4 lump pairs unnamed). Lumps without a sender are rare otherwise (12-month 4 of 651 origin-lump pairs, 24-month 0 of 1,379).
- **Prediction against result.** Precision falls where a sender mixes kinds, as predicted: the DRENT aid sender on the 24-month set (0 of 4: its next deposit is the small monthly bursary, not the next lump) and v2L-IFAM-01 (0 of 4). **Recall was predicted to be better on the long set and is worse** (38 against 56 percent). What I see without testing it: the long set's ITIGHT personas are different draws (v2L-ITIGHT-03 has 11 findable pairs and 2 hits, its balance below the buffer most of the time), and the lumps of 365 days mix a sender's tuition funding, top-ups and irregular transfers, so the median gap and size fit no single kind. Size errors are large (median 29 to 39 percent; ITIGHT 36 to 75 percent).

**b) Repeated large outflows (default next-date rule: the earliest of "a year after the latest" and "the latest plus the median gap").**

| Set | Placements (tuition) | Precision | Recall, payee with 2+ payments | Recall, all tuition payments in a horizon | First-time cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 24-month | 57 (57) | 68% (39/57) | 46% (37/81) | 46% (37/81) | 0 |
| 12-month | 9 (9) | 67% (6/9) | 26% (6/23) | 7% (6/81) | 28 |

- **Gate: fail** on the 24-month set: recall 46 percent (below 50) and precision 68 percent (below my 70). **Line b) stops: arms 4 and 5 are not run.** Nothing was tuned or changed after seeing this.
- Why, from the per-persona counts (24-month): the four summer-term personas (three terms a year, about 120 days apart: v2L-IFAM-02, -ITIGHT-03, -DHOME-02, -DRENT-01) get 39 of 44 placements right and 37 of 58 payments caught. The three two-term personas (January and September: v2L-IFAM-01, -ITIGHT-01, -DRENT-03) get 0 of 13 right and 0 of 23 caught: their gaps alternate between about 240 and 125 days, so the median gap lands between terms, and "a year after the latest" is never the next term when there is more than one term a year. The "year" rule alone placed nothing on either set, the "gap" rule alone gave exactly the default's placements.
- **Prediction against result.** High recall for the summer-term personas: yes. A second year of tuition helping: no, because the rule only looks a year after the *latest* payment, not a year after the same term. Installments: the 60-day minimum kept the second installments from counting as repeats (v2L-IFAM-02, two installments, is 12 of 12 right).
- No placement fell on a non-tuition payee (57 of 57 are tuition).

### 2026-10-11: Step 3: the arms (method and prediction, written before computing)
- **Arms run:** 0 the current forecaster; 1 random lump arrivals (step 0); 2 a) with every proposal confirmed (a naive user), random arrivals for the rest; 3 a) with only the proposals that are right on the answer key confirmed (a best-case user; an upper bound, not a result a person could get), random arrivals for the rest. **Not run: 4 (b alone) and 5 (a and b together)**, because b) failed its gate in step 2.
- Settings as before, so every arm is comparable: 20 development personas, the 40 weekly origins, 500 futures, generated histories with seed 42, weekday method, raw chances (calibration is shelved). Both sets, reported apart, never pooled. Records go to `data/backtest/arms/<set>/arm<n>` (`python -m eval.arms`); arm 0 is the development backtest already run for steps 1 and A (the same code path).
- **Sanity checks before reading anything** (the plan's). The recorded "current raw" and "prototype raw" columns (`docs/results/overnight_2026-10-10.md`, Task 4) were made before the holiday fix and exist only as pooled numbers. So I rerun arms 0 and 1 on the 12-month set with the one-line holiday fix reverted, in a scratch copy that is never committed, and require: (i) its pooled numbers equal the recorded columns, to the digits printed there; (ii) per persona, its records equal the fixed code's records everywhere except at SAL-01 and TIGHT-01 (where only 4 of 40 origins each may differ). If (i) fails for arm 1 only, the likely cause is that my rebuild of the arrivals draws in a different order from the lost prototype; I report the difference and do not compare arm 1 by digits. If the arm 0 check fails, I stop and find out why.
- **Metrics** (existing code, n beside every number): balance error at 7, 14 and 30 days and skill against "the balance stays"; lowest-balance bias and typical miss, also split into windows that contain a tuition payment (from the answer key) and windows that do not; 80 percent band coverage and width; path-level coverage; Brier score overall and per archetype; alerts at the provisional rule and at the 90 percent threshold chosen in step 1, both definitions of caught, overall and per archetype. ITIGHT is always its three development personas, -01, -02 and -03 (-04 and -05 are held out), with -01 and -03 named as known limitations (they run past -$3,000).
- **Read with suspicion:** an improvement larger than the proposal scores allow (a) is right on 82 percent of 49 proposals on the 12-month set and 69 percent of 81 on the 24-month set, nearly all at ITIGHT and IFAM); a persona that improves only through the answer key (arm 3 is an upper bound); any metric above 95 percent.
- **Prediction:** arm 2 is worse than arm 3 where false proposals exist (ITIGHT, IFAM-01 on the long set, the DRENT aid sender); arm 1 and the a) arms move the forecast towards optimism in windows with tuition (as the prototype did); they help the lowest-balance bias in windows with family money and no tuition (less pessimistic).
- **Continue only if** the sanity checks pass. **Unexpected** (write up, no further arms): an arm that makes the 30-day error or the lowest-balance bias worse for most archetypes on both sets, or a proposal-based arm (2) that beats arm 3's upper bound.

### 2026-10-11: Step 3 result: the sanity check failed in its per-persona part; the cause is found; line 3 stops
- **Check (i), pooled numbers: pass, exactly.** With the one-line holiday fix reverted in a scratch copy (never committed), the 12-month development run reproduces every number of both recorded columns (`docs/results/overnight_2026-10-10.md`, Task 4) to the digits printed there: arm 0 ("current raw": errors $667 / $1,204 / $1,901, coverage 57 / 51 / 41 / 52 percent, width $394 / $591 / $849, path 8 percent, lowest bias +$724, Brier 0.059, recall 50 percent (26 of 52), precision 77 percent, 0.041 false per 30 days) and arm 1 ("prototype raw": $666 / $1,199 / $1,881, coverage 62 / 56 / 49 / 57 percent, width $542 / $1,852 / $2,931, path 10 percent, bias +$749, Brier 0.061, recall 40 percent (21 of 52), precision 74 percent, 0.041). So the pipeline is the one that made those numbers, and my rebuild of the random lump arrivals draws exactly like the lost prototype.
- **Check (ii), per persona: fail.** I required that the fixed code's records equal the pre-fix records everywhere except at SAL-01 and TIGHT-01 (4 of 40 origins each). They differ at **16 of 20 personas, 103 of 800 origins** (IFAM-01, -02, -03 and SEM-01 at 12 origins each; DRENT-01 at 9; ITIGHT-02 at 8; DHOME-02, -03, ITIGHT-01, -03, SAL-01, -02, -03, TIGHT-01, -02 at 4; DRENT-02 at 2).
- **Why (found, no bug):** in every one of the 103 records the only difference in the placed payments is that the fix removes 1 or 2 items (106 in all), and every removed item is a second copy of a money-in payment placed on the same day for the same series. The fix (`later = [d for d in later if d > first]`) removes any later due date that shifting earlier moves onto the first placed day, which happens with weekends as well as holidays, and at every income series (pay, IFAM's allowance, wages, bursaries), not only at the two holiday paycheques the diagnosis saw (that diagnosis covered 13 of the 20 personas and counted the regular-income part only). So the plan's premise "the holiday fix is the only difference" holds; its estimate of where it shows was too narrow.
- **What I did, by the stopping rule:** I did not change the check after seeing it, and I did not read or report the arms. Line 3 stops: no arm comparison is given. The arm records were computed (arms 1 to 3 on the 12-month set and part of the 24-month set) but their metrics were not computed or looked at, apart from what the check itself printed: the fixed code's pooled numbers for arm 0 (the step 1 backtest: errors $594 / $1,058 / $1,737, coverage 60 / 56 / 47 / 57 percent, lowest bias +$710, Brier 0.057) and arm 1 (errors $593 / $1,051 / $1,715, coverage 65 / 61 / 55 / 62 percent, lowest bias +$736, Brier 0.059, recall 40 percent, precision 69 percent). These are printed by the check, not read as a comparison, and nothing was chosen from them. Arms 2 and 3 were never summarized.
- **For me to decide:** whether this explanation lets step 3 go ahead. If it does, the arm records can be rebuilt and the report made with the commands in `docs/results/overnight_2026-10-11.md` (`python -m eval.arms`, then `--report`); the method entry above stays as written.

### 2026-10-11: Step 0 result, and where the run ended
- **Step 0 result: the check passed.** With the switch off, the simulated output of v2-ITIGHT-01, v2-IFAM-02 and v2-SAL-01 (3 origins each, 100 futures, every future's path) is byte for byte what the code gave before the change; the rule-zero tests (later rows deleted, rewritten, or passed straight in) and the own-stream test pass, and mutation checks fail them. Step 3's check later showed that the rebuilt arrivals reproduce the lost prototype's pooled results to every printed digit.
- **Where the run ended:** steps 0, A, 1 and 2a passed their checks; 2b failed its gate (line b) stopped); step 3 stopped at its sanity check. The report is `docs/results/proposals_2026-10-11.md`; status, decisions made on the run's own, open questions and the commands for me are in `docs/results/overnight_2026-10-11.md`. Nothing changes in the app: every new part of the forecaster is an option that is off by default, and no page or button was changed.

### 2026-10-12: The two branches merged into `main`; a) on my real account; two decisions about the proposals run; a rule change
- **Merge.** `income-stream` (keeps-coming as the default) went into `main` first, then `proposals`. Both change the simulation inputs and `build_forecast`, so they were merged by hand: **keeps-coming stays the default**; the proposals run's random lump arrivals, a) lump proposals and b) repeated large payments stay as they were built, **off by default** (`ForecastOptions`). When random lump arrivals and keeps-coming are both on, a row that keeps-coming already draws is left out of the lump arrivals, so nothing is drawn twice. The full suite (slow tests included) passed after the merge. Four tests had to change for reasons that are not the code: tests that pin an option on the old forecaster now say `keeps=None`; one path test compared forward slashes; the real-arm folder test passes `--keeps-coming off`; and the test that checks the 12-month exports are byte for byte unchanged compared an answer key written with Windows line endings against a hash taken with Unix ones (with the line endings normalized the hash is identical, so the generator was not changed).
- **a) on my own account fails the precision bar it passed on the synthetic personas.** **Decision: keep a) as an option, off in the app; no proposal screens yet.** This used my account as development data.
- **c) "caught": the reading that matches the written definition.** A dip counts as caught if an alert episode was open at any ordinary origin before the dip, within 30 days, **even if that episode opened while the balance was already below the buffer.** The proposals run read the written rule more strictly (an episode that opened in a shortfall never counts), which added a condition that was never written down. Said honestly: I had already seen what the other reading adds (18 more caught dips at the 5 percent threshold and 9 more at 90 percent, at the personas that hover near the buffer, the very cases that motivated the change), so the choice was not blind; I made it because it matches the text of the rule, not because of those numbers. Precision, false alerts per 30 days and the shortfall episode counts do not change, because a false alert is still an alert episode that opened at an ordinary origin with no dip in the 30 days after; episodes opened while already below the buffer keep their own line. **The proposals run's threshold (90 percent) and its recall figures were computed on the strict reading and must be computed again** (tonight's Step 3).
- **The proposals run's Step 3 stop: the sanity check's expectation was wrong, not the code.** The check expected the holiday fix to show at two personas; it shows at 16 of 20, because the fix removes any later due date that shifting moves onto the first placed day, which also happens with weekends and with every income series (the same finding as in the keeps-coming result entry). The per-persona half of that check is withdrawn. The arms were not read; they are run again on the merged code, with keeps-coming as the baseline.
- **Rule change (mine): what real-data reports may show.** Replaces "reports on real data print counts only". New rule: *aggregate metrics over many forecasts may be printed in my terminal and shared; never rows, dates, names or individual transaction amounts, and never written into the repository.* Why: counts alone made it impossible to compare errors and spreads, and the scripts already print only aggregates. The printing code is not changed. What stays: nothing from my accounts is written into the repository (results here are described in words), and no automated tool ever opens, reads or runs anything on `data/private/`. `docs/PLAN.md` and the "Week 4 step 4" entry are updated to match.

## Overnight run (2026-10-12, branch `overnight-2026-10-12`)

### 2026-10-12: The run follows `docs/plans/overnight_2026-10-12.md`, on its own branch
- The run follows the plan step by step (R, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5), with its "continue only if" checks and its stopping rule, and then my Step 6 (blending with "the balance stays the same"), which I pre-register in its own entry once Steps 2 to 4 are finished. Synthetic development personas only, both sets; no held-out persona, no final test. Status after every step in `docs/results/overnight_2026-10-12.md`.
- The plan names the branch `overnight-1012`; I asked for `overnight-2026-10-12`, so that is the branch.

### 2026-10-12: R4, shortfall episodes walk calendar days (a definition change, written before the fix)
- **What changes.** `shortfall_episodes` in `eval/metrics.py` joined the observed days of a persona's 30-day windows and walked only those days, so when two windows did not touch (origins more than 30 days apart, as in a `--quick` run) a dip before the gap and one after it could be merged into one episode, or a run below the buffer could carry on across days nobody observed. From now on it walks every calendar day from the first observed day to the last: **an unobserved day ends a run below the buffer, and a dip after unobserved days is a new episode, never merged with one before them** (the 3-day merge applies only across observed days). Everything else in the episode definition stays.
- **Old and new counts on the synthetic development set** (computed with the new rule in a scratch script before the fix; records of the merged forecaster, keeps-coming on, options off, 500 futures, seed 42):

| Records | Old rule | New rule |
|---|---|---|
| 12-month set: 20 personas, 40 weekly origins (800) | 52 episodes | 52 episodes |
| 24-month set: 20 personas, 40 weekly origins (800) | 51 episodes | 51 episodes |
| 12-month set thinned to the 4 quick origins (80) | 20 episodes | 22 episodes |
| 24-month set thinned to the 4 quick origins (80) | 19 episodes | 22 episodes |

- With weekly origins the windows overlap, every day is observed (checked: no persona has an unobserved day), so the full development numbers do not move. Only runs whose windows leave gaps change: a quick run, and a real account with gaps between origins.
- **Reports that become stale:** none of the recorded full-run synthetic results (their windows overlap). Any episode count from a `--quick` run, synthetic or real, computed before this change, is stale and must not be compared with one computed after it.

### 2026-10-12: R7, known generator limitations recorded (nothing regenerated)
- **Hourly students are not all paid on Fridays.** The v2 entries say wages are paid on the biweekly Fridays. Counted on the generated 12-month histories (all 30 personas, answer key): 14 personas have hourly wages (every ITIGHT, DHOME and DRENT persona), and 7 of the 14 are paid on Mondays or Tuesdays (ITIGHT-02 and -05, DHOME-01, DRENT-01, -02, -03 and -05); the others on Fridays (moved to a Wednesday or Thursday around holidays). The review counted 7 of 13; my count of hourly-paid personas is 14. The pay weekday comes from the anchor date drawn for each persona, not from a Friday rule.
- **The away personas are paid in early December.** DHOME-03, DRENT-02 and DRENT-05 are away in July, August and December "with no wages", but each gets one or two pay deposits in the first half of December (pay for hours worked in late November falls due then). It is a property of paying a period's hours on the next pay date, not a separate bug.
- Both stay as they are: the v2 mix is frozen, and changing them would start a new set of results. They are limitations to read the results with (the weekday of pay moves the weekday baseline's pattern; the December pay makes the away months less quiet than described).
- Corrected the quoted 30-day errors of the calm archetypes in the first development backtest entry: $402-930, not $200-930.

### 2026-10-12: Step 1: b) by calendar position (method, answer key, gate and prediction, written before anything is scored)
- **Why.** The proposals run found that "the latest payment plus the median gap" fails for tuition paid in two terms (January and September): the gaps alternate between about 240 and 125 days, so the median lands between terms, and "a year after the latest" is never the next term. Its gate failed (24-month set: recall 46 percent, 37 of 81; precision 68 percent, 39 of 57). This is one new rule, not a menu.
- **The rule** (`ForecastOptions(outflow_placements=True, outflow_rule="calendar")`; the earlier rule stays `"earliest"` and is unchanged). For a payee with at least 2 big payments in the 730 days up to the origin (the big-row cutoff of the origin, the payees exactly as before), each past payment *p* proposes the date *p* + 365 days, moved to the next business day as placements already were. The placed date is the earliest proposed date after the origin and on a forecast day; if several past payments propose that same date, the latest of them gives the amount. The amount is that same-term payment's amount (not the latest payment's). Nothing is placed when no proposed date falls in the horizon. There is no 60-to-400-day gap condition: the plan's rule has none, so installments a month apart are each placed a year on. A payee seen once gets the existing first-time offer. The key is the payee and the date of the payment that proposed the date. Unchanged: the +/- 5 day jitter in the futures, "detected, not confirmed", the dated decision log (Looks right / Not this time), and everything when the option is off.
- **Nothing drawn twice.** The rows of a payee whose payment is placed (the placement's evidence) never become keeps-coming arrivals. This applies whenever placements are on (any rule); with placements off nothing changes. It matters only for a payee with 6 or more big payments a year (keeps-coming's payment floor); tuition never reaches it. Test: with ten big payments a year to one payee, keeps-coming draws them when placements are off, and none of the placed payee's rows is a member of `Arrivals` when they are on.
- **Answer key and scoring**, as in the proposals run (`eval.proposals_score`): a placement is right if a tuition payment of the generator's answer key to the same payee is posted within 10 days of the placed date and within 20 percent of its amount. Precision = right placements / placements (origin, placement pairs). Recall = tuition payments inside an origin's horizon, among origins where that payee had 2 or more big payments by the origin, that a right placement matches (origin, payment pairs). Development personas of the 24-month set; reported also apart for the two-term personas (v2L-IFAM-01, -ITIGHT-01, -DRENT-03), the three-term personas (v2L-IFAM-02, -ITIGHT-03, -DHOME-02, -DRENT-01), by archetype, and for the 12-month set (expected weak: a payment a year before a 12-month origin's horizon is before the history starts, so I expect no placements at all there). The earlier rule is scored beside it in the same run, and **must reproduce the proposals run's 24-month numbers (57 placements, 39 right, recall 37 of 81)**; if it does not, I look for the cause before reading the calendar rule.
- **Gate (the plan's):** on the 24-month set, recall at least 50 percent among payees with 2 or more payments **and** precision at least 70 percent. If it fails, I write the table and the reasons and stop line b) (arms 3 and 4 are then not run). I do not try a second rule.
- **Continue only if** the tests pass: rule zero (rows after the origin rewritten or added change nothing, in the function and in the forecaster), a decision dated after the origin is ignored and one dated on it is used, determinism with a fixed seed, and the keeps-coming exclusion above. Mutation checks: reading rows after the origin, and dropping the exclusion, must each make a test fail.
- **Prediction, written first.** Recall rises mainly at the two-term personas (the earlier rule got 0 of 13 placements right there); the three-term personas stay about as they were; precision does not fall below the earlier rule's (68 percent). Where it can go wrong: a persona whose tuition changed amount (the generator keeps tuition the same from year to year, so this set cannot show it; a known limitation recorded with the set), and installments about a month apart (the second installment a year on may fall in the horizon of a later origin and be matched to the wrong payment). **Unexpected:** precision or recall above 95 percent (I look for leakage first); fewer right placements than the earlier rule at the three-term personas.
- Command: `python -m eval.proposals_score --calendar` (both sets, 20 development personas each, 40 origins); results in `docs/results/proposals_scores_2026-10-12.md`.

### 2026-10-12: Step 1 result: b) by calendar position passes its gate on the 24-month set (too good to mean much; no leakage found)
`python -m eval.proposals_score --calendar`, 20 development personas per set, 40 weekly origins; table in `docs/results/proposals_scores_2026-10-12.md`. Counts are (origin, placement) and (origin, tuition payment) pairs; the 7 personas that pay tuition from chequing are the honest n.

| 24-month set | Placements | Precision | Recall, payee with 2+ payments | Date error, median days |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| calendar, all | 79 | 96% (76/79) | 93% (75/81) | 3.5 |
| calendar, two-term personas (3) | 23 | 91% (21/23) | 91% (21/23) | 4 |
| calendar, three-term personas (4) | 56 | 98% (55/56) | 93% (54/58) | 3 |
| earliest (the earlier rule), all | 57 | 68% (39/57) | 46% (37/81) | 1 |
| earliest, two-term personas | 13 | 0% (0/13) | 0% (0/23) | - |

- **The earlier rule reproduces** the proposals run exactly (24-month: 57 placements, 39 right, recall 37 of 81; 12-month: 9, 6 right, 6 of 23), so the scorer is the one that made those numbers.
- **Gate: pass** (recall 93 percent, precision 96 percent; at least 50 and 70 needed). Line b) continues: arms 3 and 4 run in Step 2.
- **Precision is above 95 percent, so I looked for leakage first** (a check written after seeing the number, for this purpose only): on the 7 tuition-paying personas, 280 origins, 79 placements, no evidence row was posted after its origin; adding every later row of the history (flows typed on all rows) changes no placement at any origin; moving every later row 40 days and doubling its amount changes none either. The rule-zero tests and their mutation checks pass. **No leakage found.** The number is high because the synthetic data makes the rule easy: the generator keeps tuition the same amount every year, and each term's date is drawn within 5 days of a fixed day (Jan 20, May 20, Sep 12), so a payment plus 365 days lands within 10 days of the next year's payment almost always (it is the known limitation recorded with the set). Of the 3 wrong placements, 1 is a second installment of v2L-IFAM-02 and 2 are v2L-DRENT-03 (a January payment placed from the previous January's; I did not look further). Real tuition rises and payees can change, so this says the rule does what it is meant to, not how well it would do on a real account.
- **Prediction against result.** Recall rose mainly at the two-term personas (0 to 21 of 23), as predicted. The three-term personas did not "stay about as they were": they also improved (recall 37 to 54 of 58, precision 89 to 98 percent), because "a year after the same term" is right for every term, while the earlier rule's median gap is off whenever the terms are unevenly spaced. Precision did not fall (68 to 96 percent). The 12-month set has no placement at all (a payment a year before an origin's horizon is before the history starts), as expected; 28 of its 81 (origin, tuition payment) pairs are first-time cases.

### 2026-10-12: Step 2: the arms on the merged code (method, sanity checks, drop rule and prediction, written before any arm other than 0 is run)
- **Arms** (keeps-coming on in every arm, as it is the default; raw chances; 20 development personas; the same 40 weekly origins; 500 futures; seed 42; weekday method; the 12-month and 24-month sets reported apart, never pooled):
  - 0: the forecaster as merged, options off (the baseline);
  - 1: a) on top, every lump proposal confirmed (a naive user); keeps-coming draws the rest;
  - 2: a) on top, only the proposals that are right on the answer key confirmed (a best-case user: **an upper bound**, never what a user would get);
  - 3: b) by calendar position placed by default, **only if Step 1 passed its gate**;
  - 4: a) as in arm 2 and b) as in arm 3 (**an upper bound** too), only if Step 1 passed.
  Random lump arrivals are not an arm (keeps-coming replaces them). `eval/arms.py` is rewritten for these arms (the 2026-10-11 option sets are in its history); records of arms 1 to 4 go to `data/backtest/arms-2026-10-12/<set>/arm<n>`; arm 0 is the development backtest of each set (`data/backtest/full`, `data/backtest/long/full`, the same code path).
- **"Keeps-coming draws the rest", made exact (a code change, written before it runs).** Until now a confirmed lump proposal took its sender's lumps out of the *random lump arrivals* pool only; keeps-coming could still draw the same lumps as arrivals, so a confirmed deposit would be counted twice. From now on the lumps of a sender whose proposal is confirmed (or turned down with "Not this time") never become keeps-coming arrivals either, as the evidence rows of a placed payment already do (Step 1). With the options off nothing changes (tested: an unconfirmed proposal leaves the arrivals and the futures exactly as without proposals; a confirmed one removes its sender's lumps from `Arrivals.members`; a mutation that drops the exclusion fails the test).
- **Each record of arms 1 to 4 says** how many lump proposals were confirmed and how many payments were placed at that origin (`confirmed`, `placements`, from a forecast with the same options and no futures), so a forecast that changes with nothing confirmed or placed can be found.
- **What I had seen before writing this entry** (said plainly, since arm 0 was run before it): arm 0's records of both sets were made at the start of the run (before the R fixes, none of which touches the forecaster; Step 1's change is inert with the options off). From them I computed the shortfall-episode counts for R4 (52 and 51) and, while checking R4 and R5, the pooled arm 0 metrics of the 12-month set (they are the keeps-coming column recorded on 2026-10-11, see check (i)) and the provisional rule's caught counts under both definitions: 12-month 25 opened ahead and 48 on ahead of 52; 24-month 17 and 45 of 51. Nothing of arms 1 to 4 had been run or seen.
- **Sanity checks before reading anything** (continue only if both pass): (i) arm 0's pooled numbers on the 12-month set equal the "keeps coming, with own-account" column of `docs/results/keeps_coming_2026-10-11.md`, to the dollar (and the percentages and counts printed there); (ii) arm 0's records equal those of a fresh run of `python -m eval.backtest` (keeps-coming as the default, options off) on the 12-month set, field for field, apart from the informational `version` (the commit) and the arm runner's own `arm` and `confirmed` fields. If a check fails, I find the cause and write it up, I do not change the check, and I do not read the arms.
- **Metrics, with n** (existing code): balance error at 7, 14 and 30 days and skill against "the balance stays"; lowest-balance bias and typical miss for all windows and for windows with a tuition payment, with family money and no tuition, and with neither (answer key); 80 percent band coverage and width; whole-path coverage; Brier overall and per archetype; alerts at the provisional rule under both definitions of caught, overall and per archetype; how many origins had a confirmed proposal or a placement. The 90 percent threshold of the proposals run is not printed: it was chosen on the strict reading of "on ahead" and is chosen again in Step 3.
- **Drop rule (the plan's).** An arm is *worse* than arm 0 for an archetype if its mean absolute 30-day error is more than 1 percent above arm 0's, or the absolute value of its mean lowest-balance miss is more than 1 percent above arm 0's (ordinary origins). An arm is dropped if 5 or more of the 8 archetypes are worse on either measure.
- **Prediction, written first.** a) helps the international students (ITIGHT, IFAM) a little and does nothing for SAL, SEM, TIGHT and FREE; b) helps the students who pay tuition from chequing in windows with a tuition payment (where the lowest balance is the largest remaining optimism) and is neutral elsewhere; a) and b) together are best in windows with both. **Unexpected, so write it up and stop that line:** an arm worse than arm 0 for most archetypes; an answer-key arm (2 or 4) *worse* than its non-key version (1 or 3) on most archetypes; any persona whose forecast changes at an origin with no confirmed proposal and no placement.
- Commands: `python -m eval.arms --arms 1 2 [3 4] --set both`, then `python -m eval.arms --report` (writes `docs/results/arms_2026-10-12.md`).

### 2026-10-12: Step 3: the alert threshold again, on raw chances (method and prediction, written before computing)
- **Records:** arm 0 of Step 2 on the 12-month set (the forecaster as merged, keeps-coming on, options off; 20 development personas, 40 origins, 500 futures, seed 42, weekday method), which passed both Step 2 sanity checks. The episode rule is R4's (calendar days). The 24-month set is not used to choose.
- **Definition of caught:** both are printed; the choice uses "on ahead" in the reading recorded on 2026-10-12 (an alert episode that opened while the balance was already below the buffer counts from the first ordinary origin at which it is still on). This is what `eval/metrics.py` computes on `main` (`caught_on_ahead` takes every alert episode, wherever it opened).
- **Objective, unchanged:** the highest recall (on ahead) subject to precision of at least 70 percent and at most 0.1 false alerts per 30 days, ordinary origins only; ties to the higher precision, then the higher threshold; thresholds 2, 5, 10, 15, ..., 95 percent (chance only, no median clause); chosen leave one persona out as before (the threshold chosen on the other 19 applied to the 20th); the provisional rule (above 20 percent, or the median lowest balance below the buffer) printed as its own row. `python -m eval.thresholds_raw` now writes `docs/results/thresholds_raw_2026-10-12.md` (the 2026-10-11 file is not overwritten).
- **Stop checks (the plan's three):** the new caught set contains the old at every threshold; precision, false alerts per 30 days and the episode counts are the same under both definitions; the leave-one-out folds choose no more than three different thresholds. If one fails, I report the table and the diagnosis and choose no threshold.
- **A reading I had to make.** The plan says "stop checks (as in the proposals run)" and then lists three. The proposals run had a fourth: recall rising by more than 5 points at thresholds of 80 percent and above. The plan's own prediction ("nearly equal at high ones") says it expects that not to happen, but it does not list it as a check. I take the three listed checks as the stop checks, compute the fourth and print it, and **if the fourth holds I say plainly that under the stricter reading no threshold would have been chosen**, so the choice can be undone in the morning. From R4's check I already know arm 0's provisional-rule recall under the two definitions (25 and 48 of 52), so I expect the fourth condition to hold: the new reading counts alerts that stay on through a dip at the hovering personas, at every threshold.
- **Prediction, written first (the plan's):** the chosen threshold is high (90 percent last time), and recall under the new reading is higher than under the old at low thresholds and nearly equal at high ones. My own expectation, from the numbers above, is that the gap stays large at high thresholds too.
- Nothing changes in the app.

### 2026-10-12: Step 3 result: the three stop checks pass and the objective picks 20 percent; the proposals run's fourth condition holds (development, nothing changes in the app)
Arm 0 of the 12-month set: 20 development personas, 800 origins (726 ordinary), 52 shortfall episodes. Full curve in `docs/results/thresholds_raw_2026-10-12.md` (`python -m eval.thresholds_raw`).

| Rule | Recall, opened ahead | Recall, on ahead (2026-10-12 reading) | Precision | False alerts per 30 days |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5 percent | 48% (25/52) | 92% (48/52) | 48% (21/44) | 0.136 |
| 20 percent (chosen) | 48% (25/52) | 92% (48/52) | 70% (21/30) | 0.053 |
| 50 percent | 46% (24/52) | 87% (45/52) | 81% (21/26) | 0.030 |
| 90 percent | 58% (30/52) | 77% (40/52) | 96% (23/24) | 0.006 |
| 95 percent | 54% (28/52) | 67% (35/52) | 95% (21/22) | 0.006 |
| provisional rule (20 percent or median low under the buffer) | 48% (25/52) | 92% (48/52) | 70% (21/30) | 0.053 |

- **Stop checks (the plan's three): none holds.** The new caught set contains the old at every threshold; precision, false alerts and episodes are the same quantities for both definitions; the leave-one-out folds chose 3 thresholds (20, 35 and 40 percent; at most 3 allowed).
- **Chosen on all 20 personas: 20 percent.** Recall (on ahead) 92 percent (48 of 52), precision 70 percent (21 of 30, exactly on the bar: one more false alert would rule it out), 0.053 false alerts per 30 days, lead time 20.3 days (median 22). It gives the same alerts as the provisional rule here (the median clause adds nothing on these records). Leave one persona out: recall 88 percent (46 of 52), precision 74 percent (20 of 27), 0.041 false alerts per 30 days.
- **The proposals run's fourth condition holds** (recall on ahead more than 5 points above opened ahead at 80, 85, 90 and 95 percent: for example 77 against 58 percent at 90). As written before computing: **under the stricter reading that keeps it as a stop check, no threshold would be chosen.** Which reading stands is my call.
- **Prediction against result: wrong.** The plan predicted a high threshold and recall nearly equal under both definitions at high thresholds. Under the 2026-10-12 reading of "on ahead", an alert that stays on through a dip and its recovery counts for the next dip, so low thresholds now gain the most (DRENT-03 +8, TIGHT-03 +7, TIGHT-02 +4, ITIGHT-03 +3, ITIGHT-01 +1 at 20 percent), and the highest recall within the constraints is at the low end, where precision just reaches 70 percent. The gap stays at 10 to 20 points at high thresholds, as I expected in the method entry from arm 0's provisional-rule counts.
- How I read it: "on ahead" rewards an alert that is simply on for long stretches at the personas that hover near the buffer, so a lower threshold wins recall without paying in false alerts (a false alert is still counted per alert episode, and a long episode is one). That is a property of the definitions, not evidence that 20 percent is a good threshold for a person. n is small: 52 episodes at 8 personas. Nothing changes in the app.

### 2026-10-12: Step 6: blending with "the balance stays the same" (my pre-registration, written before anything is computed)
- **Why.** On my account the forecast doesn't beat "the balance stays the same" yet (candidate experiment 2 of the keeps-coming entry). This tests whether pulling the forecast towards that baseline helps, on synthetic development personas first. It runs only after Steps 2 to 4 were finished, whatever their outcome.
- **The blend.** For each horizon day h (1 to 30), blended balance = naive + w(h) x (forecast - naive), where naive is the balance at the origin. The same blend is applied to every simulated future, so the band (10th to 90th percentile), each future's lowest balance, the median lowest balance and the chance of dipping below the buffer all come from the blended futures. The single-path estimate is blended with the same w(h) (it is not used to fit). w(h) is between 0 and 1.
- **The fit.** For each day h, w(h) is the value on a grid of 0.00, 0.01, ..., 1.00 that gives the lowest mean absolute error of the middle future (the blended p50) against the observed balance on that day, over the ordinary origins (not already below the buffer) of the fitting personas; ties go to the larger w. Then the 30 values are **smoothed to be monotone: non-increasing in h** (the closest non-increasing sequence in squared error, by pool adjacent violators, every day weighted equally), clipped to [0, 1]. I chose non-increasing because the forecast's skill against the naive baseline falls with the horizon, so the weight on the forecast should not rise with it; a low-order curve was the other option and is not used.
- **Evaluation: leave one persona out** on the 20 development personas of each set, the 12-month set and the 24-month set separately (never pooled): for each persona the weights are fitted on the other 19 and applied to all 40 of its origins. Compared with the merged forecaster with keeps-coming, options off (arm 0): balance error of the middle future at 7, 14 and 30 days, skill against the naive baseline, lowest-balance bias and typical miss, 80 percent band coverage (7, 14, 30 days and all days) and width, whole-path coverage, Brier score of the chance of dipping below the buffer, and alerts under the current definition of caught (the provisional rule, the app's alert engine, "on ahead" in the reading of 2026-10-12; "opened ahead" printed beside it), with precision and false alerts per 30 days. Per archetype too. n (personas, origins, episodes) with every number.
- **Data.** The blend needs every future's daily balance, which the saved arm 0 records do not keep, so `python -m eval.blend` reruns arm 0 of each set with paths (`data/backtest/blend/12` and `.../long`; the same code path as the calibration step's run). Its records must equal arm 0's field for field (apart from the informational commit and the arm runner's own fields) before anything is read; if not, I find out why first.
- **The weights carried forward** are fitted on all 20 development personas of each set and saved in `docs/results/blend_weights.json` (both sets, with the per-day fit before smoothing).
- **Real data, evaluation only.** `python -m eval.blend apply --real <folder>` applies the saved weights (both sets' weights, side by side) to a real backtest folder with paths (`python -m eval.backtest_real --paths`) and prints summary numbers before and after, with my own buffer and at $0. It refuses a folder outside `data/private`, fits nothing on real data, and writes nothing. It is listed in the morning commands.
- **Prediction, written before computing (mine):** skill improves at 30 days; coverage drops somewhat, because the blended bands are narrower. **Not adopted in the app** whatever comes out; I decide after checking it on my account.

### 2026-10-12: Why recurring placement misses and adds payments on my account: method, written before the script (branch `recurring-check`)
- **Why.** In the real-account error breakdown, some of the payments the forecast placed did not happen (missed) and some real payments of known series were not placed (extra). Before changing detection or placement I want to know where they come from. My account is development data; I read this while looking at it, so nothing here is an evaluation, and the fix (if any) is a separate decision.
- **What it is.** `python -m eval.recurring_check`, run by me. It opens the database read-only, uses the same weekly origins as the real backtest, builds the forecast of each origin with no futures (the placed payments do not depend on them) and the old forecaster (`keeps=None`; recurring placement is the same either way), and matches the placed payments with the real payments of the 30 days after with the **same rule as `eval.diagnose_real`**: within one series, one to one, nearest day first, within 10 days. It must give the same totals as that script for the same origins (a test checks it on synthetic personas). It prints counts and shares only: no name, date, amount or row, and writes nothing.
- **Definitions.** *Placed*: a payment the forecast puts on one of the 30 days (all series, in and out). *Matched*: it has a real payment of the same series within 10 days. *Missed*: placed, no match. *Extra*: a real payment of a series (as the series stand 30 days after the origin; a label, the forecast never sees it) that no placed payment matched. Origins "not already below the buffer" are shown beside all origins, as before.
- **Broken down, for the placed payments (placed, matched, missed, and the share missed in each group):**
  1. the series' status at the origin (detected, confirmed, added by me) and the status it has today (detected, confirmed, added by me, rejected, not in today's list); decisions are replayed as of the origin, so at past origins a series is almost always "detected", and "today" shows what I decided later; active or ended today;
  2. cadence (weekly, biweekly, semimonthly, monthly, yearly) and amount type (fixed, variable); whether it is a rent-rule series;
  3. **days since the series' last real payment at the origin** (under 45, 45 to 90, over 90, none found), and the same measured in periods of its own cadence (up to 1.5 periods, 1.5 to 3, more than 3): this is the test of "is it placing series that had already stopped?";
  4. what the series' payments are: card purchases, bill payments, e-Transfers, or other (the majority of the flow reasons of its payments; "none found" for a series with no payments);
  5. how the payment was placed: from the pattern; expected on the first day because it was due and not seen yet (the cautious rule, DECISIONS 2026-10-06); or a rent window.
- **For the missed payments, one more split:** the same series *did* pay in the 30 days but more than 10 days from the placed day (a wrong date, counted both as missed and as extra), or it did not pay at all.
- **For the extra payments:** by the same series attributes (as they stand 30 days after the origin), and **was the series known at the origin?** Three groups: not detected at the origin (a series first found later); detected and placed, but more than 10 days from the real payment (the wrong-date twin of a miss); detected but not placed in the window (its next date was outside it, or it had ended). For all of them, days since the last real payment at the origin, and how many payments of the series came before the origin (none, 1, 2, 3 or more: a series with 2 was one payment short of being found).
- **Checks, fixed now.** On synthetic personas, with tests: the totals equal `eval.diagnose_real`'s for the same origins; every breakdown adds up to its total; what is known at the origin (the placed payments and their attributes) does not change when every later row is rewritten (rule zero); the output contains no date, name or amount.
- **Not claimed.** The 10-day matching window decides what counts as "missed" and "extra"; a stricter or looser window would move payments between the two. A series' key can change when its detection changes, which would turn one series into a miss and an extra; that case shows up as "not known at the origin" with a payment of the same cadence, and is not separated here.
- **What I expect, written first.** On my account the misses are concentrated in series that had stopped (a subscription I cancelled, a series with long gaps) and in payments assumed overdue on the first day; the extras are mostly series first detected after the origin. If instead most misses are series that paid within the last cadence period but a few days off, the fix is placement dates, not detection.

### 2026-10-12: Recurring placement on my account: what the check showed (in words), and a proposed fix (not approved, nothing built; branch `recurring-check`)
**What the check showed (my account, 92 origins, summary numbers run by me, none written here).** Its totals match the error breakdown. The misses fall into a few kinds of series, weekly card-purchase series among them; most extra real payments came from series not yet detected at the origin.

**A caution about the weekly result, before it is used.** A weekly series made of card purchases is found as a cluster of purchases of about one amount, and its key contains the median of that amount (`merchant|out|<name>|<amount>`, `lookahead/recurring.py`). When the amounts vary a little (card habits do), the median moves as purchases arrive, the key changes, and the same weekly habit is counted as a missed placed series and, 30 days later, as an extra series "not detected at the origin". The weekly misses and extras of the check are exactly what that would look like, so part or all of the weekly result may be a measurement artifact of matching by key, and not payments that never happened. **First step, a diagnostic only:** match the placed and real payments by series *family* (the key without the trailing amount: merchant and direction) as well as by key, and count how many of the weekly misses and extras disappear. If most do, (a) below is fixing a measurement problem, and the real problem is that the key is not stable; I would then propose a stable key instead. This check is a new option of `eval/recurring_check.py` (my own file) and changes no forecast.

**Where the rows of a recurring series go, today.** In `build_forecast`, `in_series` is the set of rows of every series that is not rejected (detected, confirmed or added by me, active or ended). These rows are skipped by the everyday-spending stream, the irregular-deposit stream and the big-row rules (`series_rows` in `lookahead/forecast/spending.py`), so each is counted only through its series: as a placed payment on its expected dates if the series is active, and nowhere if it has ended or is rejected-then-restored. Rows of a series I rejected ("not recurring") are not in `in_series`, so they are in the everyday stream. The forecast reads the plain flow type of each row (a card purchase is "everyday"); the "recurring outflow" override is for the pages only.

**Proposed fix, behind a switch, off until I approve.** A pure function in a new module `lookahead/forecast/recurring_filter.py`, a field `recurring_fix` on `ForecastOptions` (default none), and a few lines in `build_forecast`; nothing in `eval/backtest*`, `eval/metrics.py` or the Time Machine files, and the proposals branch's changes stay untouched. It decides, for each series, from rows posted on or before the forecast date only (rule zero):
- **(a) Weekly series of card purchases that I did not confirm or add by hand are not placed.** "Card purchases" = most of the series' payments are card purchases; weekly = cadence weekly. **Where their rows go:** they leave `in_series`, so they are in the everyday-spending stream (the weekday average and the weekly draw) exactly like any other card purchase, and they are no longer placed: nothing is lost (the rows are in the stream) and nothing is double counted (the series is not placed). A weekly series of bill payments or e-Transfers is untouched.
- **(b) A series whose last real payment is more than 1.5 periods of its cadence before the forecast date is "possibly ended": not placed, and never placed on the first day as "due and not seen yet".** Periods: weekly 7 days, biweekly 14, monthly 30, yearly 365; so the limits are more than 10.5, 21, 45 and 547 days. **Semimonthly is left alone.** **Where their rows go:** they stay in `in_series`, so they are in neither stream and not placed. Putting them back in the everyday stream would let a cancelled payment be drawn at random by the weekly draw. It comes back by itself when a new payment arrives (the last payment is then recent). Today a series is placed until two due dates in a row pass unpaid; (b) stops placing it after about one, which is what the check showed.
- **My additions, to accept or refuse:** (b) does not apply to a series I confirmed or added by hand, nor to a series with a rent rule (a late rent is expected, and rent has its own overdue logic); a series with no payment found (added by hand, none yet) is not "possibly ended". Decisions are replayed as of the forecast date, so in a backtest nothing is confirmed yet and the exemption applies only to the live forecast.
- **The risk, stated plainly:** (b) is optimistic if a series is only late. A confirmed or hand-added series is exempt for that reason.

**Prediction, written before anything is run.**
- *Synthetic development personas (n beside every number; 12-month set; 20 personas, 40 origins, 500 futures, seed 42; the original and the new "caught"):* personas with no weekly card series and no series that stops are **identical to the digit** to the current forecaster. The generator plants clean schedules, a few subscriptions that change price, and few if any cancellations, so I expect most personas to be identical and the rest to move by little; synthetic data can show that the fix does no harm, not that it helps (the same as for keeps-coming).
- *My account (development, not evaluation):* the number of placed payments falls, mainly weekly card series and series that had stopped; the missed share falls a lot; the recurring part of the 30-day error and the lowest-balance pessimism shrink; the number of extras does not fall by much (they are series found late), unless the family check above shows that most weekly extras are an artifact. Risk: a few more dips missed if a stopped series was in fact only late.
- **Unexpected, so write it up and do not tune:** an archetype worse by the usual rule (more than half of the 8 archetypes worse by more than 1 percent on the 30-day error or on the absolute lowest-balance miss, judged for (a), for (b) and for both); any persona that changes without a weekly card series or a possibly ended series.

**How it will be evaluated, and the commands for my account** (written now, built after approval; each variant in its own folder, never mixed): synthetic runs for off, (a), (b), both, with a table like the keeps-coming one; for my account: `python -m eval.recurring_check` with the fix off and on, `python -m eval.diagnose_real` with the fix off and on, and a backtest plus `eval.metrics` for off, (a), (b), both, compared with keeps-coming as it stands on the merged code (the "off" run, made now, is the baseline: the earlier keeps-coming folders predate the merge). My changes to the scripts I own (`eval/recurring_check.py`, `eval/diagnose_real.py`) and new runners; `eval/backtest*` is not edited.

### 2026-10-12: Recurring placement: the family check first, (b) approved and built behind a switch, and what happens to my decisions when a series key changes
**Decisions (mine).** (1) The family-matching check runs before (a) is built; (a) waits for its result. (2) (b) is approved as written, with the exemptions: a series I confirmed or added by hand, and a series with a rent rule, is never "possibly ended"; neither is a series with no payment found. (3) Check, on made-up data, what a key change does to my saved decisions; report, do not fix.

**The family check (method, before the code).** `python -m eval.recurring_check --by-family` prints, beside the usual numbers, the same matching with a series' *family* instead of its key. The family of a key is the key without a trailing amount: `merchant|out|<name>|<amount>` and `e-transfer|out|<token>|<amount>` have the family of their first three parts, `own transfer|out|<amount>` of its first two; a key without an amount is its own family. A placed payment and a real payment are the same payment if they are one to one in the same family, nearest day first, within 10 days (the same rule otherwise). It prints, for all origins and for the ordinary ones: placed, matched, missed and extra by key and by family, for all series and for weekly series, and for weekly card series; and, of the payments missed or extra by key, how many are matched by family (they were the same series under a new key); and how many series known at an origin have, 30 days later, the same family under a different key. Counts and shares only. Synthetic tests check that a series whose key changes is matched by family and not by key, and that the two matchings are identical when no two keys share a family. (They are not claimed to be "never fewer": nearest first over a pool shared by two keys can in rare cases pair fewer payments than two separate pools.)

**What a key change does to my decisions (found on made-up data; not fixed).** A "card cluster" series (a weekly habit among other purchases at one merchant) has a key that ends in the median amount of its purchases. In a made-up case the key moved from `...|1204` to `...|1200` as purchases arrived. Decisions are stored against the key, and `current_series` ignores a decision whose key no longer exists, except a confirmation (it keeps a snapshot "confirmed once: it stays"). Result, the day after the key change, one decision at a time:
- **confirm**: not lost, but **duplicated**: two series for the one habit, the old confirmed snapshot (active, no payments attached) and the new detected one, and both are placed, so the habit is counted **twice**.
- **reject** ("not recurring"): lost. The rejected habit comes back, detected and placed.
- **end** ("it ended"): lost. The series is active again.
- **rename** and **category**: lost.
- By the code (`current_series`, `if d.key not in series: ... continue`) the same holds for a link ("continues"), a rent rule and an edit; I did not build a case for those three.
This is pinned by tests that show the lost and duplicated cases and are marked as known failures until it is fixed. It also means a decision I made can silently stop applying, and a confirmed weekly habit can be placed twice; both push the forecast pessimistic, and both make "series no longer in today's list" larger. A stable key (the family, with the amount kept inside the series) is the likely fix and is a separate decision.

**(b) built behind a switch, off by default.** `ForecastOptions.recurring_fix` (default none) selects `RecurringFix(possibly_ended=True)`; the rule lives in `lookahead/forecast/recurring_filter.py` and `build_forecast` uses it only when the option is set. A series is *possibly ended* when, as of the data's last day, its last real payment (the latest of its evidence payments posted on or before that day) is more than 1.5 periods before it: weekly 7 days, biweekly 14, monthly 30, yearly 365 (limits strictly above 10.5, 21, 45 and 547 days). Semimonthly is left alone. Never possibly ended: a series I confirmed or added by hand (or edited), a series with a rent rule, a series with no payment found. A possibly ended series is not placed (so never placed on the first day as "due and not seen yet"); its rows stay out of the everyday stream (they stay in `in_series`); it comes back by itself with a new payment. Rule zero: only payments posted by the data's last day. Tests: the limits on both sides, each exemption, semimonthly, no payment found, a payment arriving brings it back, rows after the data's last day are ignored, off is identical to today, on with nothing possibly ended is identical.

**Runners for the evaluation (new files; `eval/backtest*` and `eval/metrics.py` are not edited).** `eval/recurring_fix_eval.py` runs the synthetic development backtest for `off` and `b` (own folders) and compares them with the keeps-coming table and the same drop rule (more than half of the 8 archetypes worse by more than 1 percent on the 30-day error or on the absolute lowest-balance miss). `eval/recurring_fix_real.py` runs my real backtest with the fix off or on in its own folder (`data/private/backtest/recurring-fix-<variant>/full`), and `eval.metrics` reads those folders. `eval.diagnose_real` and `eval.recurring_check` take `--recurring-fix off|b`. Only I run anything on my data.

### 2026-10-12: (b) possibly ended series, synthetic result (development personas; built behind a switch, off by default, not run on my data)
20 development personas, 726 ordinary origins (800 in all), 500 futures, seed 42, raw chances; the current forecaster (keeps-coming on) against the same with (b). Table in `docs/results/recurring_fix_2026-10-12.md`. The drop rule fixed beforehand (more than half of the 8 archetypes worse by more than 1 percent on the 30-day error or on the absolute lowest-balance miss) **is not triggered**: no archetype is worse on the 30-day error; two are worse on the lowest-balance bias by the 1 percent rule (SEM $69 to $73, TIGHT $64 to $65, tiny amounts).
- **Overall (ordinary origins):** 30-day error $1,727 to $1,725; 7 and 14 days $593 to $592 and $1,056 to $1,052; lowest-balance bias +$716 to +$703; Brier 0.058 to 0.060; coverage over all days 58 percent both. Alerts at the provisional rule: recall 25 of 52 both; precision 21 of 30 to 22 of 32; false alerts per 30 days 0.053 to 0.059 (two more alert episodes, one right and one false). Small changes in both directions.
- **The prediction was half wrong.** I predicted most personas would be identical and the rest would move little. The result: (b) placed fewer payments at 34 of 800 origins, and **only 5 of 20 personas are identical** (DHOME-01, DRENT-01, FREE-01, IFAM-02, SAL-02); the other 15 differ at 1 to 4 of their 40 origins. The synthetic personas have more series that pause than I assumed (away months, breaks, exam periods, a changed pattern). The sizes of the changes are small, as predicted.
- **Were the removed payments real misses?** At the 34 changed origins (b) removed 93 placed payments. **80 of them did not happen** (they were misses: weekly 20, biweekly 35, monthly 25); **13 did happen** (9 monthly payments out, 2 monthly and 2 biweekly payments in): (b) removed a payment that then arrived. So about 86 percent of what it removes is right, and about 14 percent is a payment lost that the forecast would otherwise have had; the second kind pushes the forecast optimistic at those origins. That is the cost of the rule, and why a series I confirmed or added is exempt.
- **Checks passed:** off equals the current forecaster field for field; with the switch on and nothing possibly ended the forecast is identical; the limits, the exemptions, semimonthly, "no payment found" and rule zero are tested, and four deliberate mutations of the rule were each caught by a test.
- **How I read it.** (b) does what it was written to do and costs little on synthetic data, where it has little to act on; whether it helps depends on my account. Not run on my account yet. (a) waits for the family check.

- **(b) on my account, against the current forecaster:** nothing that matched is lost.

Nothing is lost (the rows are in the stream) and nothing is counted twice (the series is not placed). A risk: a real weekly habit I would want placed (a standing weekly payment by card) is returned to the everyday stream; it is then forecast on average, not on its day, and I can confirm it to have it placed. If the check passes, (a) becomes the default and the commands for my account are `recurring_fix_real --fix a` and `--fix both`.
