### 2026-10-12: Does deposit timing depend on the time since the last deposit? Method, written before the script (branch `income-timing`, read-only diagnostic)
**The recurring-placement fix on my account, in words (development data, summary numbers run by me, none written here).** With (a) and (b) together nothing that matched is lost and weekly placements are gone. Open, for later: semimonthly series, and payments placed on the first day as "due and not seen yet".

**Why this check.** The random arrivals of keeps-coming draw each day independently at a constant rate. If a deposit that comes about every month is more likely to arrive 3 or 4 weeks after the previous one than 1 week after it, a constant rate gets the shape wrong: it makes two deposits in a month or none for months more likely than they are. That could spread the futures and pull the middle one down while the mean stays right. This check only measures; it changes no forecast, and what to do about it (a due-date model) is a separate decision.

**What it is.** `python -m eval.income_timing`, run by me. It opens the database read-only, uses the same weekly origins as the real backtest plus the week after each, and prints counts, shares and gap statistics only: no row, date, name or amount, and it writes nothing.

**Two populations of deposits, both read the way the forecaster would at each date (rule zero: rows posted by that date; series and decisions as of that date).**
1. **Recurring-size deposits** (what keeps-coming treats as arrivals): the money-in members of `keeps_coming_arrivals` at that date, with own-account transfers counted, as now.
2. **All deposits of $500 or more in no series:** money in of a kind the forecaster counts (income or transfer type), not netted, at least $500, in no series at that date.

**The probability that the next deposit comes within 7 days.** For each weekly origin *t* and each population: the previous deposit is the latest of the population at *t* (an origin with none is counted apart); the days since it are grouped 0 to 7, 8 to 14, 15 to 21, 22 to 28, 29 to 35, and 36 or more. The event is that at least one deposit of the population, as it stands at *t* + 7, was posted in the days *t* + 1 to *t* + 7 (the future is used only to label the outcome). For each group: n (origins), the share with the event, and **the probability implied by the constant rate of the current model** for the same origins, 1 minus (1 minus the rate) to the 7th power, where the rate is the number of deposits of the population in the last 365 days before *t* divided by the days observed in that window (the rate keeps-coming uses; for population 2 the same construction). Overall row too. Windows of consecutive weekly origins do not overlap, but one deposit can sit in several groups' "previous", so n is origins, not independent deposits.

**Gaps.** The days between consecutive deposits of each population, using every deposit that was in the population at some origin (a row counted once): median, the two quartiles, and the share of gaps of 20 to 35 days; with n.

**By type.** The same tables for incoming e-Transfers, own-account transfers and credit memos separately, each with its own previous deposit, its own event and its own constant rate, printed only when the type has at least 15 deposits in the population (otherwise "too few").

**Checks, fixed now.** On synthetic personas, with tests: the groups cover every origin with a previous deposit exactly once; the implied probability is 1 minus (1 minus the rate) to the 7th; a population at a date does not change when every later row is deleted (rule zero); the output has no date, name or amount.

**Prediction, written first.** For population 1, if deposits come about monthly, the share arriving within 7 days is low in the first two groups, rises in the groups from 22 to 35 days, and is higher there than the constant rate implies, and lower than it in the first two groups; the median gap is in the 20 to 35 day range. For population 2 (more e-Transfers and top-ups, less regular) the shape is flatter. If the shares are flat across the groups and close to the constant rate, the timing does not depend on the time since the last deposit, and a constant rate is a fair model.
**Not claimed.** Origins are overlapping observations of one account; the result is a description, not a test. The types are the bank's transaction types, and an e-Transfer can be income or a transfer from another person's account.

### 2026-10-12: Proposal (not approved, nothing built): keeps-coming deposits drawn from the account's own gaps, given the days since the last deposit
**Problem.** The arrivals of keeps-coming deposits are drawn independently each day at a constant rate. Real income is often not like that: a monthly deposit is unlikely right after the previous one and likely some weeks later. A constant rate then puts two deposits in one month or none for months too often. The futures spread out, and the middle future sits below the mean (the "low point" part that grew in the error breakdown).

**Design: a renewal-style draw of the next gap.** For the combined stream of recurring-size deposits (all types together, not per type), at a forecast date *t*:
- **The gaps.** The recurring-size deposits of the last 365 days (the same rows keeps-coming uses, from rows posted by *t*) sorted by posting day; the gaps are the days between consecutive *distinct* days (several deposits on one day are one arrival day; a day still has at most one arrival, as now). The days since the last one: *e* = *t* minus the day of the latest.
- **First arrival.** Draw the next gap *g* at random from the past gaps that are **longer than *e*** (strictly: the account has already waited *e* days without one). The first arrival is on forecast day *g* − *e* (at least day 1).
- **Later arrivals.** Each next gap is drawn from **all** past gaps, with replacement, until the horizon is passed.
- **Sizes** as now: each arrival's size is drawn at random from the observed sizes. Payments' arrivals stay as they are (constant rate), the stream stays in the futures "with deposits" only, "no other money expected" turns it off, a spending cut does not touch it.
- **Fallback to the constant rate, for the whole future,** when there are too few gaps: fewer than **6 past gaps** (7 deposit days) in the window, or fewer than **2 past gaps longer than *e*** (nothing to draw the first gap from). I propose 6 and 2 because quartiles of fewer than 6 gaps are not stable and a first gap drawn from a single value is not a draw; they are not tuned on my account and I would report a sensitivity (4, 6 and 8) on the synthetic personas only. A forecast says in its assumptions which of the two it used.
- **Rule zero.** Only deposits posted on or before *t*, so only gaps observed before the origin; a test deletes every later row and checks the gaps, *e* and the draws are unchanged. Own random stream per future (named by the seed, the future and "renewal"), so no other draw moves and a scenario still differs from its original only by the change.
- **Built as an option** (`KeepsComing(renewal=True)`), off until approved; `renewal=False` is today's forecaster exactly (a test).

**Cheap step first (read-only).** Extend `eval.income_timing` with a column "what the renewal draw implies" next to "what the constant rate implies": for each origin, among the past gaps longer than *e*, the share that are no longer than *e* + 7. If that column follows the real shares, the draw captures the timing before any forecaster change; if not, I do not build it. No forecast changes.

**Prediction, written first.**
- *Synthetic development personas, both sets (12-month and 24-month; 20 personas, 40 origins, 500 futures, seed 42, raw chances, both definitions of caught):* personas with no recurring-size deposits, or too few gaps, are **identical to the digit** to the current default. The generator's irregular family transfers come every 15 to 45 days (a uniform gap), whose chance of an arrival in the next 7 days does depend on the time since the last one, so ITIGHT (and possibly FREE) change: the 30-day band narrows, the middle future rises towards the mean, the 30-day error and the lowest-balance bias move little (a few percent), and the Brier score about the same. Where the generator's gaps are close to memoryless (random weekly weights), no change.
- *A risk:* when *e* is longer than most past gaps the first arrival comes soon (the model thinks it is due), which can look optimistic right before a long gap; the fallback counts show how often.
- **Drop rule (fixed now):** the same as for keeps-coming and the recurring fix: dropped if more than half of the 8 archetypes are worse by more than 1 percent on the 30-day error or on the absolute lowest-balance miss, on either set. **Unexpected, write up and do not tune:** a persona that changes without recurring-size deposits; fewer than half of the origins of a persona with such deposits using the renewal draw; the band wider than today's.

**Evaluation.**
1. Synthetic, both sets: `renewal` against the default, with the usual table, per archetype, tuition and family-money windows, the identical personas, the share of origins that used the draw and the share that fell back, and the sensitivity of the minimum (4, 6, 8) on the synthetic sets.
2. A direct check of the timing: the same hazard table as `eval.income_timing`, but computed from the model's simulated futures for the origins of each synthetic persona (the share of futures with an arrival in the next 7 days, by days since the last deposit) against what really happened; constant rate and renewal side by side.
3. My account: `eval.backtest_real` with the option in its own folder, `eval.metrics` with my buffer and at $0 (error, skill, coverage, width, lowest-balance bias, Brier, dips caught), `eval.diagnose_real` (the "low point" part), counts of origins on the draw and on the fallback; development data, so not an evaluation.
4. If it passes, it becomes the default only on my approval.

### 2026-10-12: Real-account results on the merged `main` (in words): b) by calendar position stays off; blending not adopted; on my account the forecaster doesn't beat the simple baseline yet
**What I ran (my account, development data; summary numbers, none written here).**
- **The default forecaster on the merged code** (keeps-coming, recurring fix (a) and (b)): identical to before.
- **b) repeated large payments by calendar position (arm 3): worse on my own account.** **Decision: b) by calendar position stays OFF by default** (it is a switch, never on in the app). It passed its synthetic gate only on data that makes it easy (fixed tuition, fixed term dates). **For later:** place a repeated large payment only if I confirmed it, or if it was seen in two or more past years (not one year earlier); to be pre-registered before it is built.
- **Blending with "the balance stays the same" using the synthetic 12-month weights: not adopted.** The weights were fitted on synthetic personas, not on my account; see the proposal below.

### 2026-10-12: The renewal draw's read-only step approved: what the draw implies, next to the real shares (method, before the code)
Approved: extend `eval.income_timing` with a column; no forecast changes. For each origin and population (and type, as before), from the same deposits the table already uses and rows posted by the origin only (rule zero): the past gaps are the days between consecutive *distinct* posting days of the population in the last 365 days; *e* is the days since the latest one. **What the renewal draw implies** for the next 7 days: among the past gaps longer than *e*, the share that are no longer than *e* + 7. **Fallback:** when there are fewer than the minimum number of past gaps (6, the proposal's default, settable with `--min-gaps`), or fewer than 2 gaps longer than *e*, the draw uses the constant rate, so the implied chance is the constant rate's and the origin is counted as a fallback. Printed per group: n, the real share, the constant rate's chance and the renewal draw's chance; the number of origins on the draw and on the fallback; and, for each model, the mean absolute difference between its chance and the real share over the groups, weighted by n. **A minimum other than 6 is accepted only for a database outside `data/private`**: 4, 6 and 8 are checked on synthetic personas only and never tuned on my account. Counts and shares only. **Prediction:** on my account the renewal column follows the real shares more closely than the constant-rate column and most origins are on the draw; on the synthetic personas the uniform 15 to 45 day transfers of ITIGHT give a chance that rises with the days since, which the draw follows. If the renewal column is no closer to the real shares than the constant rate, the draw is not built.

### 2026-10-12: Proposal (not approved, nothing built): per-account adaptive blending with "the balance stays the same"
**Problem.** The blend of Step 6 uses weights fitted on synthetic personas, where the forecast beats "the balance stays the same" at every horizon, so the weights stay near 1. On my account the forecast doesn't beat "stays" yet. The right weight depends on the account, and an account can say so itself: it has its own record of how the forecast did against "stays".

**Design.** The blend and the fit are those of Step 6, for each horizon day *h*: blended balance = naive + *w*(*h*) x (forecast - naive), naive = the balance at the origin, applied to every simulated future; *w* is the value on the grid 0.00 to 1.00 (step 0.01, ties to the larger) with the lowest mean absolute error of the blended middle future against the observed balance, over ordinary origins. What changes is whose origins it is fitted on.
- **Track record (rule zero).** At the origin *t* the account's own earlier weekly origins *s* whose day *h* is already observed, that is *s* + *h* <= *t*, not already below the buffer at *s*. For day *h* that is *n*(*h*) origins (more for short horizons than for 30 days). Only rows posted by *t* are used. The forecast at each past origin is the replay of the forecaster in force, as of *s* (it is not what was shown that day if the code has changed since; said on the page).
- **The account's own weight** ŵ(*h*, *t*): the same grid fit on those origins.
- **Minimum.** Fewer than **8** past origins for day *h* (two months of weekly origins): no account-specific weight, the synthetic weight *w*0(*h*) is used as it is.
- **Smoothing.** (1) Shrinkage towards the synthetic weight: *w*(*h*) = (*n*·ŵ + *k*·*w*0) / (*n* + *k*) with **k = 12** pseudo-origins; weekly origins overlap, so 12 is about three independent 30-day windows and the account's own record needs to be longer than that to dominate. (2) Then the 30 values are made non-increasing in *h* (pool adjacent violators, as in Step 6) and clipped to [0, 1]. (3) Recomputed once a week, at each Monday origin, never between. No decay of old origins now (a change of regime, such as a new tuition pattern, takes a while to show; noted as a limit).
- **The synthetic weight** *w*0 is the average of the 12-month and 24-month sets' weights, refitted on whichever forecaster is in force when this is built (the saved weights were fitted on the forecaster of 2026-10-12, before the recurring fix and any renewal draw; "the fit is valid for the forecaster it was made on").
- **Built as an option, off by default**, and never in the app until I approve.
- **Pre-declared variant: keep the spread.** Scaling every future towards "stays" also shrinks the band, which is why coverage fell in Step 6 (48 to 40 percent at 30 days on synthetic data) and will fall more when *w* is small. The variant moves only the centre and leaves each future's distance from the centre alone: blended future = naive + *w* x (middle - naive) + (future - middle). Both variants are evaluated; the primary one is the scaled blend (the one already run), and coverage decides between them, said before looking.

**Evaluation.**
1. *Synthetic development personas, both sets (12-month and 24-month, never pooled; 20 personas, 40 weekly origins, 500 futures, seed 42):* run sequentially, so origin *i* uses only the persona's own earlier origins; *w*0 from the other 19 personas (leave one persona out, as in Step 6). Compared with arm 0 (the forecaster with no blend) and with the global blend (Step 6's), on the Step 6 table: error at 7, 14 and 30 days, skill, lowest-balance bias and typical miss, coverage and width, whole-path coverage, Brier, alerts with both definitions of caught, per archetype, n beside every number; and the path of *w*(30) by origin number (how fast an account moves away from *w*0). **Drop rule (fixed now): adaptive against the global blend, dropped if more than half of the 8 archetypes are worse by more than 1 percent on the 30-day error or on the absolute lowest-balance bias, on either set.**
2. *A mechanism check with constructed errors (unit tests):* a forecast that is always right gives *w* near 1; one that is always far off gives *w* near 0; fewer than 8 past origins gives exactly *w*0; the weights are non-increasing; a later row never changes an earlier weight (rule zero by deleting every row after *t*).
3. *My account (development data, not an evaluation):* from my backtest folder with paths, replayed sequentially in the same way (origin *i* uses only earlier origins of mine): error, skill, bias, coverage and width, Brier, dips caught with my buffer and at $0; by history length (26 or more weeks against under 26); the path of *w*(30) over my origins.

**Prediction, written first.**
- *Synthetic:* the adaptive weights stay close to the global ones (the synthetic accounts' own records say the forecast is good), so the results are within about 1 to 2 percent of the global blend; personas where the forecast is weaker than "stays" move most. The drop rule is not triggered.
- *My account:* the account's own weight should move the forecast towards "stays"; the effect on dips caught needs care.
- **Unexpected, write up and do not tune:** weights that jump by more than 0.2 from one week to the next; a persona with a good forecast whose *w*(30) stays below 0.8 for most of its origins; the keep-spread variant's coverage below the scaled one's.

**How it interacts with the renewal draw, if both are adopted.**
1. **Order.** The adaptive weights measure the forecaster in force, so the renewal draw is decided first and the weights (the synthetic *w*0 and the account's replays) are made on the forecaster that will ship. Adopting the blend first and the draw later would leave the weights fitted to the wrong forecaster.
2. **They attack the same symptom from two sides.** The renewal draw removes a source of swings (arrivals at the wrong times); the blend damps whatever swings remain. If the draw is right, the account's track record improves and *w*(30) rises towards the synthetic weights; if *w*(30) stays low even with the draw, the forecaster still adds swings my balance does not make, which is a finding about the forecaster and not about the blend. So the path of *w*(30) with and without the draw is itself a diagnostic and is reported.
3. **Evaluated as 2 x 2** (constant rate or renewal draw, with or without the adaptive blend), on both synthetic sets and on my account, before either is made a default; each of the four with the same table.
4. **No double credit.** Both decisions use my account as development data; the final held-out test is the only test of the pair, run once, with the choices made before it.

### 2026-10-12: Bug fix (review of 2026-10-11): the keeps-coming rate divided by days on which a deposit could not have qualified; **forecasts change, saved results must be run again**
**The bug.** Keeps-coming counted its arrivals (rows with 2 or more earlier rows of similar size) over **all observed days** of the last 365. The first two rows of a stream can never be members, so the days before the third one were counted as days on which an arrival could have happened and did not. A stream that comes every 30 days (6 deposits in 180 days: the 3rd to the 6th are members) got 4 / 180, about one in 45 days, instead of one in 30. The rate, and so the amount of money the futures expect, was too low for every stream that began inside the year, and by more the younger the stream.

**The fix.** The rate is members over the days from the first member (the third deposit of its kind) to the forecast date, inclusive: 4 / 120 = one in 30 in that example (a test). **A guard I added and that the review did not ask for:** with fewer than 2 members the observed days are kept, because one arrival over the few days since the third deposit would give a rate near 1 a day (a forecast of a deposit almost every day for a month); one member says nothing about a gap. Payments use the same days (they come back only at 6 or more a year, so they always have 6 members). Which fits the "from the third similar deposit on" of the review is for me to say; the guard is what I would change first if it is not wanted.

**What this changes.** Every forecast of an account with a stream of recurring-size deposits or payments that began inside the last year expects more of that money. **Everything saved from the forecaster before this fix is made with another rate and must be run again** (the development backtests of both sets, the arms, the Model report's columns, my account's backtest folders, the Time Machine snapshots): the version of the saved results is raised in the same review (the settings of the forecaster are recorded in them from now on), so the code itself refuses the old ones and says so. Results written in this log before this entry stay as they were, for what they were made with; comparisons against them are no longer like for like. The renewal draw's gaps are taken from the same member rows and do not move.

**Prediction (not measured yet).** On the synthetic sets the personas with young streams (the family-transfer personas) change most: the middle future and the mean rise a little; the 30-day error and the lowest-balance bias move by a few percent and nothing becomes worse than it was in a way that matters. On my account little should change. To be measured on the development personas and on my account after the merge, before anything else is built on the old numbers.

1. **`eval.lump_diagnose` printed real dates** (first and last posting, the window dates, "days before the anchor"). Counts only now; a test finds no date in its output.

They all use `eval.backtest.current_default_dir()` now. **Any comparison made before this against "arm 0" or the metrics default read the old forecaster's records.**

4. **Small numbers are not printed** from a real account: a median or gap statistic needs 10 or more values and a group 5 or more rows, else "too few to show" (`eval/small_n.py`; diagnose_real, income_timing, proposals_real); `eval.big_money` prints a range, not the value, for a cutoff that is 5 times an odd median of fewer than 10 rows (that median is one real row).

10. **Small:** recurring fix (a) needs a strict majority of card purchases, as recorded (before, a tie could count); stale docstrings (the options' defaults, lump proposals retired, the arms example); `--held-out --only <development persona>` saves with the development records; the salt file is created owner-only (the mode has no effect on Windows, where the folder's permissions apply).
11. **Docs:** the plan's week 4 row, the horizon rule the code implements (a fixed 30 days, 2026-10-06; the 2026-09-30 entry stays as history) and the README limitation about large deposits.

**What has to be run again after this is merged** (everything made with the old rate or the old versions): the development backtests of both sets, the arms, the blend weights (they were fitted on the old forecaster), the threshold study, the Model report's synthetic column, my account's backtest folders, the Time Machine snapshots. The renewal draw's evaluation on the branch `renewal` was started before the rate fix. The draw does not use the rate (it draws from the gaps), so after the fix the default arm moves and the draw arm does not: **that comparison has to be run again on the merged code before the draw is judged.**

### 2026-10-12: The renewal draw: built behind a switch (off by default), and how it is evaluated (method, before the results)
**The read-only step (development data).** The draw is built, **behind a switch, off by default**; nothing about the forecaster in the app changes.

**What was built (as proposed, with one change of place).**
- The switch is `ForecastOptions(renewal=True)` (the proposal said `KeepsComing(renewal=True)`; it moved to the options, next to the other experimental parts, on my instruction). `renewal=False`, the default, is today's forecaster exactly: a test compares nine forecasts of three synthetic personas (100 futures, every path kept) with digests taken on the merged code before any change.
- **The gaps** are the days between consecutive distinct posting days of the rows keeps-coming draws (the recurring-size deposits of the last 365 days, only rows posted by the forecast date). Because a deposit is "recurring-size" only if it has 2 or more earlier rows of similar size, the first two deposits of such a stream are never in it: an account needs 9 such deposits in a year to have 6 gaps. (The read-only step used the same rows, so its fallback counts say the same thing.)
- **The draw**, per simulated future, from its own random stream (named by the seed, the future and "renewal", so no other draw moves): with *e* the days between the last deposit day and the end of the data, the first gap is drawn from the past gaps longer than *e*, the first arrival is on forecast day gap minus *e*; each later gap is drawn from all past gaps, until the horizon. Sizes are drawn from the observed sizes, as before. At most one arrival a day (gaps are 1 day or more).
- **Fallback to the constant rate, for the whole future:** fewer than 6 past gaps, or fewer than 2 past gaps longer than *e*. The forecast records which one it used (`draw`, `fallback`, or empty when there are no recurring-size deposits). "No other money expected" turns the draw off exactly as it turns off the constant-rate arrivals (it sets the rate to 0). Payments' arrivals are unchanged. Nothing in the app pages was touched.
- **Tests** (`tests/test_renewal_draw.py`): the gaps and both fallbacks; the first arrival comes only after a gap longer than the wait and later arrivals use any gap; a deposit is certain when due and impossible right after the last one in a constructed case; with too few deposits the option gives the constant rate to the digit; **rule zero**: a database with every later row deleted gives the same gaps, the same last deposit day and the same futures at two origins; the scenario "no other money" is the same as zero rate. **Mutation check, disclosed:** the rule-zero test fails when the later rows are let in at both places that filter by date (`build_forecast`'s query and `keeps_coming_arrivals`), but **not when only one of the two is broken**, because each filters on its own (defence in depth); a leak at only one place is therefore not caught by this test alone, and the existing keeps-coming tests are what cover each place.

**Evaluation, synthetic development personas (20 per set; the 12-month set first, then the 24-month set; never pooled; 40 weekly origins, 500 futures, seed 42).**
- Arms of `eval/arms.py`: **5** the default as merged, run again now; **6** the renewal draw (6 and 2); **7** and **8** the draw with a minimum of 4 and of 8 gaps (the sensitivity, synthetic only; nothing about the minimum is tuned on my account). **Arm 5 is a fresh run because the saved baseline in `data/backtest/full` is older than the merged code (it was made before keeps-coming; a check of three personas found its forecasts differ from the current default's).**
- Same table as the arms: error at 7, 14, 30 days, skill, lowest-balance bias and typical miss, coverage and width (30-day width compared), whole-path coverage, Brier, alerts with both definitions of caught, per archetype, tuition and family-money windows, the origins on the draw and on the fallback, with n beside every number.
- **Drop rule (fixed before, as for keeps-coming and the recurring fix):** the draw is dropped if more than half of the 8 archetypes (5 or more) are worse by more than 1 percent on the 30-day error, or on the absolute lowest-balance bias, on either set.
- **Prediction (recorded in the proposal, restated):** personas without recurring-size deposits, or with too few gaps, are **identical to the digit** to arm 5. ITIGHT (the generator's family transfers come every 15 to 45 days, a uniform gap whose chance of an arrival in the next week depends on the days since the last) and possibly FREE change: the 30-day band narrows, the middle future rises towards the mean, the 30-day error and the lowest-balance bias move by a few percent, the Brier score about the same; where the generator's gaps are near memoryless, no change. The drop rule is not triggered.
- **Unexpected, written up and not tuned:** a persona that changes without recurring-size deposits; fewer than half of the origins of a persona with such deposits using the draw; the band wider than today's. The report counts all three.
- **Not part of this run, still to do:** the proposal's direct check of the timing (the share of futures with an arrival in the next 7 days by the days since the last deposit, constant rate against draw, from the model's own futures). It is a check, not a drop criterion.
- **For my account (development data, not an evaluation):** the backtest with the switch on against the current default in its own folder, the metrics with my buffer and at $0, and `eval.diagnose_real` with it on, run by me; counts of origins on the draw and on the fallback. If it passes, it becomes the default only on my approval, and adaptive blending is decided after it (design recorded above).

### 2026-10-16: The renewal draw: the rule for making it the default, fixed before the comparison is run
Proposed, to be confirmed by me before any run. It adds an adopt rule to the drop rule of 2026-10-12 above; the drop rule stays.

**What is compared.** Arm 6 (the draw, with its fixed minimum of 6 past gaps) against arm 5 (the default, run again on the merged code). Arm 5 must equal arm 0 record for record, else something is wrong and nothing is read. Arms 7 and 8 (4 and 8 gaps) are a sensitivity check only; the minimum is not tuned. Sets: the 20 development personas of the 12-month set and of the 24-month (long) set, never pooled, then my account. Same seed, 40 weekly origins, 500 futures. The held-out personas are not touched.

**Metrics.** Main: the 30-day error and the absolute lowest-balance bias, per archetype (8 archetypes, equal weight; n beside every number). Also: the coverage of the 80 percent band at 30 days, the band's width, the Brier score of the shortfall chance, and the alert numbers at the provisional threshold (these last two are information, not gates).

**It becomes the default only if all of these hold:**
1. **Unchanged where it should be.** A persona with no recurring-size deposits, or whose origins all use the fallback, is identical to arm 5 to the digit. If not, it is a bug and I look for it before reading anything else.
2. **No archetype hurt.** On each set, no archetype is worse than arm 5 by more than 1 percent on the 30-day error or on the absolute lowest-balance bias. (Stricter than the drop rule's 5 of 8.) No single persona is worse by more than 5 percent on either; any that is gets written up.
3. **A real gain.** On each set, at least 2 archetypes are better by more than 1 percent on one of the two main measures, and the average over the 8 archetypes is better on both. A gain in an archetype where fewer than half of the origins used the draw does not count (it is noise or something else).
4. **Honest band.** In no archetype is the 80 percent coverage further from 80 than arm 5's by more than 2 points, and the band is not wider than arm 5's by more than 10 percent.
5. **Not fragile.** If arm 7 or arm 8 flips the direction of the average gain on a main measure on either set, the gain depends on the minimum and the draw stays off.
6. **My account can only veto.** The aggregate 30-day error, the absolute lowest-balance bias and the coverage must not be worse than the default's by more than 1 percent (coverage: by more than 2 points). It is one account, development data and a case study, so a good result there never decides anything alone; the counts of origins on the draw and on the fallback are shown with it.

**It stays off if** any of 1 to 6 fails, or the result is a tie. Simpler wins ties. Anything unexpected (a persona that changes without recurring-size deposits, a wider band, fewer than half of the origins of a counted archetype on the draw) is written up and not tuned away.

**Prediction.** Only the family-transfer personas (uniform gaps) gain, by a few percent; the rest are identical. If so, condition 3 may fail on one set, and the draw stays off. That is an acceptable result.

**After the rule.** Whatever it says, the adaptive blend and the threshold are decided after, on the result. The final held-out test is run once, with the choices made before it.

### 2026-10-16: Plan for the demo branch (not started): no committed databases, snapshots remade
The Time Machine's what-if box opens `data/demo/timemachine/<persona>.db` (`scenario_at`), so the 7 databases there are used, not leftovers. They were force-added past the rule against committing `*.db`. The plan, to be done after the renewal draw is decided and before `demo` is merged:
1. The page builds a persona's database on first use from the generator (`_import_persona`, a temporary folder, cached), and the 7 `.db` files leave git. The first what-if per persona takes a few seconds longer.
2. The committed test that checks the files exist checks that the builder works instead; `docs/DEPLOY.md` and `docs/DEMO.md` are updated.
3. The saved steps (`.jsonl`, `.balances.json`, `.meta.json`) are remade once with the merged forecaster (`python -m lookahead.timemachine_snapshots --out data/demo/timemachine`, about 7 minutes). The committed ones say `logic: 2`, so the merged code would refuse them.
4. `python -m lookahead.demo reset` rebuilds only `data/synthetic/demo.db`, which git already ignores; it needs no change.
Why this order: the snapshots depend on the forecaster's default, so remaking them before the draw is decided would mean doing it twice.

### 2026-10-17: The renewal draw stays off by default (decided by the rule of 2026-10-16)
I ran the comparison on the merged code: the 20 development personas of the 12-month set and of the 24-month set, 40 weekly origins, 500 futures, arms 5 (default), 6 (the draw), 7 and 8 (4 and 8 gaps). Arm 5 equals arm 0 at all 1,600 records. The report, synthetic numbers only, is `docs/results/renewal_2026-10-12.md`. The rule I fixed before the run decided it:
- **Passed:** the draw changes nothing where it should not (condition 1), no archetype is worse by more than 1 percent (condition 2; the 24-month ITIGHT bias is up 0.97 percent, close to the limit), and the arms with 4 and 8 gaps move the same way (condition 5).
- **Failed condition 3, no meaningful gain.** Only ITIGHT changes at all; the other seven archetypes are identical to the digit. ITIGHT's 30-day error is down 0.16 percent (12-month) and 1.36 percent (24-month), its lowest-balance bias is slightly worse in both, and on the 12-month set the draw is used at only 11 of 58 origins.
- **Failed condition 4, the band.** The draw narrows ITIGHT's 80 percent band, which is already too narrow: coverage at 30 days falls 7.0 points (12-month) and 6.2 points (24-month), against a limit of 2.
- **One persona drives most of the gain:** on the 24-month set v2L-ITIGHT-02 improves by 11 percent on the error; the other two ITIGHT personas move by well under 1 percent.
- Condition 6 (my account) was not run: conditions 3 and 4 already decided it, so it stays off and my account is not used to argue for it.
The code stays behind `ForecastOptions(renewal=True)`, off by default, and the tests stay. The draw is not tuned or retried on these results. The prediction of 2026-10-16 (only the family-transfer personas change; condition 3 may fail) held.

### 2026-10-17: Re-picking the alert threshold on the new synthetic records: the procedure, fixed before it is run (proposed, **ON HOLD**)
The 20 percent in the app is provisional, and the study that chose it (2026-10-12) was made on records of the forecaster before the keeps-coming rate fix. The records are now new (the development backtests of both sets, run again on 2026-10-17). **On hold (2026-10-17):** the threshold is picked on the final forecaster, and I am going to try improving the forecaster first (an experiment plan proposed on 2026-10-17, to be written here with its adopt rules once I confirm it). The procedure stays as written and is not run until the forecaster is settled; then it is confirmed again or changed before it runs. The blend refit is folded into the online-combination experiment of that plan and is not done on its own.
Nothing below has been run.
- **Records.** The development personas only. The 12-month set (800 origins, 20 personas) is where the threshold is chosen, as before, so the numbers stay comparable. The 24-month set is a second look: the chosen threshold is applied to it without choosing again. The held-out personas are not opened. My account is not used: it is one account, and the draw's result already showed I do not tune on it.
- **Rule studied.** Alert when the share of futures below the buffer is above the threshold, or the median lowest balance is below the buffer; raw chances, thresholds 2 percent and 5 to 95 percent in steps of 5; ordinary origins only (those already in shortfall are on their own row). Not the calibrated chances (calibration stays shelved and is not refitted).
- **What is chosen.** The threshold with the highest recall of shortfalls "on ahead" (the 2026-10-12 definition) among those with precision of at least 70 percent and at most 0.1 false alerts per 30 days. **Ties:** higher precision, then the higher threshold (the existing `choose`). Chosen leave one persona out, and also on all 20 together; the leave-one-out row is the honest number.
- **Stop checks, fixed before.** The three of the plan (the new caught set contains the old; precision, false alerts and episodes are the same quantities for both definitions; at most 3 different thresholds across the folds), with the fourth (recall rising by more than 5 points at 80 percent and above) printed as information. If one holds, no threshold is chosen and I am told why.
- **A change needs a margin.** The curve is flat at the top (recall barely moves from 2 to 20 percent), so a different pick is not news by itself. The app moves off 20 percent only if the leave-one-out result of the new pick catches at least 2 more shortfalls than 20 percent does, with the two constraints still met; otherwise 20 percent stays. 52 episodes in 8 personas is small, and I will say so beside every number.
- **The second look.** On the 24-month set the chosen threshold must still meet the precision bar and the false-alert limit. If it does not, I say so and do not change the app.
- **What I will see.** `python -m eval.thresholds_raw` writes the report: the curve (both recalls, precision, false alerts per 30 days, lead times, n beside each), the chosen row, the leave-one-out row with each fold's threshold, the stop checks and the fourth as information, the 24-month row, and a plain sentence saying whether the app's number changes. Nothing in the app changes by itself; a change is my decision after reading it.
**Blend weights (the adaptive blend with the balance-stays baseline).** `docs/results/blend_weights.json` was fitted on the forecaster before the rate fix, so it is stale, and its "not adopted" result was reached with the old numbers. They should be fitted again, but only as a new check, and nothing is adopted from it without its own rule. That rule must be written and confirmed before the refit is run, as for the draw: the measures (30-day error, lowest-balance bias, coverage, Brier, alerts), the sets (both synthetic sets, leave one persona out, never pooled), what counts as better, the limit on harm per archetype, what the blend does to the bands (the first blend lowered coverage, so a coverage condition like the draw's condition 4 is needed), my account as a veto only, and what keeps it off. The refit also needs the saved paths of both sets, which are large; I will say how much space and time it takes before starting. Calibration stays shelved and is not refitted.

### 2026-10-17: Plan to improve the forecaster: five experiments, each with its adopt rule fixed before it is run
The threshold is picked on the final forecaster, so I am trying to improve the forecaster first. Nothing below is built or run yet except where a step says so. Common rules for every experiment:
- **Rule zero.** Every feature, window and weight used for a forecast on date *t* comes only from data posted on or before *t*; each experiment says how. A weight learned online uses only origins whose 30 days are already over.
- **Who decides.** The synthetic development sets (12-month and 24-month, never pooled) and Berka (development accounts) decide. My account can only veto: not worse than the default by more than 1 percent on the 30-day error or the lowest-balance bias, or by more than 2 points on coverage. If synthetic and Berka disagree, the experiment stays off. A tie stays off.
- **Common conditions** (as for the renewal draw): no archetype worse by more than 1 percent on the 30-day error or the absolute lowest-balance bias; no persona worse by more than 5 percent; arm 5 equals arm 0 first; where an experiment moves the bands, the 80 percent coverage may not end further from 80 than the default's by more than 2 points in any archetype.
- **Splits fixed before results.** Berka accounts are split into development and held-out by a hash of the account id; the held-out accounts and the held-out personas are opened once, at the end, with the choices made before. Every variant I try is counted in the final report.
- **Suspicious results.** A number that looks too good is checked for leakage before it is believed. No new libraries.

**1. Baselines and scores (adopts nothing; it fixes what "better" means).** A random-walk baseline: the balance at the origin plus the 30-day paths of balance change in windows that ended on or before the origin (the 365 days before it), giving a band and a dip chance. Scores per archetype with n: CRPS (exact, from the 500 futures, the baseline's windows, and the point forecast of "balance stays"), Brier skill of the dip chance against both baselines, a reliability curve of the dip chance (is a 20 percent chance followed by a dip about 20 percent of the time?), and the dips caught at the same precision. The saved futures are rerun once and 19 quantiles per day (5 to 95 percent) are saved with them; the rerun must equal arm 0. If the forecaster does not beat the random walk on average at 30 days on both CRPS and Brier, that is written down as a finding.

**2. Berka (PKDD'99), the second dataset (a gate, adopts nothing).** Downloaded into an ignored folder, never committed, not redistributed (its licence is not stated). Adapter: each row becomes a transaction whose description is built from the codes (operation, k_symbol, partner bank). The categorizer and merchant resolution are not evaluated on it. First counts only: transactions per month per account, months of history, gaps, the split of flow between small everyday-style spending and monthly payments. The account filter, the buffer rule and the development/held-out split are proposed from those counts, and I confirm them before anything is scored. It is a Czech bank of the 1990s, a different population from a student's account; it checks generalization and does not stand in for my account.

**3. Net-flow anchoring.** The futures' average net flow over the 30 days is pulled towards the trailing realized net flow (a trimmed figure, so one big deposit does not set it), adjusted for the scheduled items already placed, past data only. Adopt only if, on each synthetic set, at least 2 archetypes are better by more than 1 percent on one main measure and the average over the 8 archetypes is better on both; the common conditions hold; on Berka development the mean 30-day CRPS improves by a margin I fix after the Berka gate and before the run, with no loss in dips caught at the same precision. It fails if it only flattens the forecast: skill against "balance stays" must improve too.

**4. Online combination with "balance stays".** Candidates: (a) the fixed 50/50 average, (b) one global weight fitted across the development personas (leave one persona out), (c) per-account weights learned online from past origins whose 30 days are over (exponentially weighted by past loss). This replaces the separate blend-weight refit. Adopt a candidate only if it beats both pure forecasts on the average over archetypes on CRPS and on Brier, on both sets and on Berka development, with the common conditions (coverage included, since the first blend lowered it); and (c) is adopted over (a) and (b) only if it beats the better of them clearly on the same measures. Otherwise the simpler one stays, or none. Needs saved futures for each set.

**5. Balance-aware simulation.** Everyday spending is resampled from past weeks that started from a similar state (balance against the buffer, days since income, days to rent), nearest neighbours, past weeks only. The synthetic generator has a cut-back rule (everyday spending halves when the available balance less bills due in 7 days is under $100) in every persona and balance-triggered family transfers in persona C only, which is not a development persona. So synthetic data can show only that the method works when the dependence exists, plus a negative control (it must not hurt where there is none); Berka decides. Adopt only if the common conditions hold on both synthetic sets, it is not worse on the negative control, and it wins on Berka development by a margin fixed after the Berka gate.

**6. Adaptive conformal bands: deferred.** The app shows a dip chance, not an interval, so a corrected band would not fix what it says. Experiment 1 measures whether the dip chance is honest; if it is not, the correction to look at is for the chance or for the lowest balance, and I decide then.

**Order.** 1, then the Berka gate, then 3, 4, 5 on the same footing. Thresholds, calibration and the blend refit wait for the result.

### 2026-10-17: Berka: the choices I made from the gate's counts, and the split (fixed before any forecast)
I confirmed these answers after reading the gate (`docs/results/berka_gate_2026-10-17.md`):
1. **Accounts.** Primary: 24 or more months of history, 4 or more non-mechanical transactions a month (not interest, not the statement fee), no quiet stretch over 90 days: 1,387 accounts. Sensitivity: the same with 2 or more a month, minus the primary ones (2,085 more).
2. **The dip line.** Berka has no personal buffer. In every report the line is called "fell below one month's outflow" (not a buffer), it is 100 percent of the account's median 30-day outflow over the 365 days before its first origin (fixed per account, past data only), and 50 percent is the sensitivity. **It was chosen from dip counts only** (the gate's table of how often a balance fell below a candidate line), **not from any forecast result.** One run serves both lines because each record keeps every future's lowest balance.
3. **The split.** `docs/berka_split.json`, made by `python -m eval.berka_split` and committed before any forecast was run. An account is development or held-out by one bit of a hash of its id; inside each pool the accounts that pass a filter are ordered by that hash and the first 200 are taken, for the primary and for the sensitivity group (no account in two lists). Code that checks or tunes anything reads the development lists through `development_accounts()`; the held-out lists are for the one final test, and `assert_development_only` refuses them. I read the whole file once to count and then drop everything except the development accounts; no held-out account is looked at.

**Adapter choices, made before any forecast and not tuned:** (a) amounts are in koruna, and the forecaster has absolute amounts in it (big money is a deposit or payment of $500 or more), so koruna are turned into cents at 5 cents per koruna. **This is a scaling choice, not an exchange rate**: it only puts Berka's amounts in the range the forecaster's absolute amounts were written for, it is not tuned, and no result is read in Canadian dollars. Without it nearly every withdrawal would be set aside as "big money". (b) Each row becomes an export row with a description in our vocabulary: cash and card withdrawals are "abm withdrawal" (everyday), the household, insurance and loan payments and other transfers out "bill payment" with the partner bank and account in the text so a series is recognised, fees "service charge", cash deposits "deposit", incoming transfers and interest "credit memo". (c) Same-day rows are put income first, and the balance after each row is the running total from the opening balance (the file's own balances drift by up to 0.2 koruna from interest rounding and list same-day rows in a wrong order at times; the report says how far the running total is from the file's closing balances). (d) 40 weekly origins per account, the last 30 days before the account's last posting, so each origin has 30 observed days.

**What the first Berka run is:** "does it run and look sane" on the development accounts: how many accounts the forecaster runs on (the gate needs 90 percent or more), how the forecast is made up (recurring placements against everyday spending), and crashes, counts only and no scores. It also holds the truncated-future test: a forecast from the full database equals the forecast from a database cut at the origin.

### 2026-10-17: Berka "does it run" check: what it found, a bug in my adapter, and how Berka results will be reported (pre-declared before any scoring)
Report: `docs/results/berka_sanity_2026-10-17.md`. Counts only; no forecast was compared with what happened; no held-out account was read.
- **Primary group (4+ a month): gate passed.** All 40 origins ran for 200 of 200 development accounts (the gate needed 90 percent), 8,000 of 8,000 origins gave a forecast, no crashes. **Sensitivity group (2+ a month): ran, flagged, informational only.** Same run counts, but 14.7 percent of origins have a zero everyday-spending estimate, and 11 accounts have more than half of their origins at zero. It decides nothing.
- **Zero everyday estimate in the primary group: 6.4 percent of origins (512 of 8,000), in 83 of 200 accounts, flagged (the line is 5 percent).** Most are not about cash in a recurring series: 447 of the 512 had no cash withdrawal in the 8 weeks at all, so what is left is the monthly payments (placed as recurring series) and fees. Only 0.5 percent of cash withdrawal rows are in a series. The baseline's 8-week window is short for accounts this sparse. **I do not tune it for Berka**: that would change the forecaster, which is its own experiment.
- **Pre-declared reporting.** Every Berka score is reported for all origins and, separately, for the zero-estimate origins, with the number of accounts as well as origins (those origins cluster: 83 accounts in the primary group). **Adopt rules use the all-origins score only.** The zero-estimate stratum can only veto, with the shared thresholds: not worse than the default by more than 1 percent on the 30-day error or on the lowest-balance bias, or by more than 2 points on coverage.
- **Pre-declared second result.** Primary results are also reported without the 16 development accounts that have a series made mostly of cash withdrawals at some origin (listed in `docs/berka_cash_series_accounts.json`, fixed now). All 200 accounts stay in the main result.
- **A bug in my adapter, found by a check I ran on the two sensitivity accounts that drifted by 700 and 1,100 koruna.** I had guessed the file was inconsistent. It is not: in the file, no day closes more than 1 koruna away from the previous close plus the day's total. The adapter took the account's opening balance from the wrong row when two rows share the first day and the larger one is listed second. It now takes the candidate nearest to zero among the first day's balances less the day's total (a test covers it). After the fix no development account in either group has a nonzero opening balance, the worst drift against the file's closing balance is 1.2 koruna (2.2 before for the primary group, which was not affected), and the two accounts were run again; the zero-estimate counts did not change.

### 2026-10-17: Experiment 3 (net-flow anchoring): the adopt rule, fixed before it is run
Not run yet. It follows the plan of 2026-10-17 and its common rules (rule zero, synthetic and Berka decide, my account can only veto, the shared thresholds, held-out data opened once at the end).

**What it changes.** The forecast's average unscheduled net flow over the 30 days is pulled towards the trailing realized one. *Unscheduled* means all money in and out except (a) rows that belong to a recurring series (they are placed on their own dates and stay as placed) and (b) rows the forecaster already sets aside as big one-time money or as a pass-through. **No other trimming is used**: no median, no percentile, no winsorizing, no cap on a single row; the set-asides are the forecaster's own rule (at least $500 and 5 times the typical row), applied to the trailing rows exactly as to the forecast. This is fixed now and not tuned.
- **Trailing figure** U_trail: the sum of the unscheduled rows posted in the 91 days ending on the origin, divided by 91. **Forecast figure** U_fc: the mean over the futures of the unscheduled net flow in the 30 days, as the simulation makes it (spending, fees, irregular deposits and keeps-coming arrivals, no scheduled items), divided by 30.
- **The shift.** Every future's balance on forecast day *h* is moved by λ × (U_trail − U_fc) × *h*. The band, each future's lowest balance and the dip chance are then read from the shifted futures.
- **Minimum history.** The trailing figure is used only when the account has at least 91 days of history before the origin **and** at least 20 unscheduled rows in those 91 days. Below either, the forecast is left unchanged. These origins are covered by condition 1.
- **Two variants are tried: λ = 1 and λ = 0.5.** The 91-day window and the trimming above are fixed and not tuned. Both variants are counted in the final report as variants tried. λ = 1 is the one that can be adopted; λ = 0.5 is the sensitivity of condition 9.

**Compared.** Arm 5 (the default, run again) against the anchored forecast, on the 20 development personas of the 12-month and of the 24-month set (never pooled; 40 weekly origins, 500 futures, seed 42), and on the Berka primary development accounts (200 accounts, 40 origins each, all origins). **Arm 5 equals arm 0 at all 800 records of each set** (checked on 2026-10-17, and again by the experiment 1 rerun); if that ever fails, nothing is read.

**It becomes the default only if all of these hold:**
1. **Unchanged where it should be.** Origins below the minimum history, and origins with no unscheduled rows, are identical to the default to the digit. If not, it is a bug and I look for it first.
2. **No harm on synthetic.** On each set, no archetype is worse by more than 1 percent on the 30-day error or on the absolute lowest-balance bias; no persona is worse by more than 5 percent on either.
3. **A real gain on synthetic.** On each set, at least 2 archetypes are better by more than 1 percent on one of those two measures, and the average over the 8 archetypes is better on both. A gain in an archetype where fewer than half of the origins were moved does not count.
4. **Honest band.** In no archetype does the 80 percent coverage end further from 80 than the default's by more than 2 points, and the 30-day band is not wider than the default's by more than 10 percent.
5. **Not just flatter.** The mean CRPS skill against "balance stays" (over archetypes) must not fall, and the Brier skill against "balance stays" must not fall.
6. **Berka, primary group, all 200 accounts, all origins.** The mean 30-day CRPS (each account's mean over its origins, accounts weighted equally) must fall by at least **1 percent** against the default, **and** the lower end of a 95 percent bootstrap interval for that relative gain must be above 0. The bootstrap resamples accounts, not origins, because the 200 accounts are the independent units and weekly origins overlap; fixed seed 2026, 2,000 draws.
7. **Berka, the other measures.** The 30-day error is not worse by more than 1 percent; the 80 percent coverage is within 2 points of the default's; the Brier score of the dip chance ("fell below one month's outflow") is not worse by more than 1 percent; and the dips caught at the same precision (the 70 percent bar) do not fall, if the 200 accounts have at least 30 shortfall episodes, else this one is informational only.
8. **Vetoes, with the number of accounts as well as origins.** The Berka zero-estimate origins (the shared thresholds: 1 percent on the 30-day error and the lowest-balance bias, 2 points on coverage), and the Berka primary result without the 16 mostly-cash accounts (the direction of the CRPS gain must hold). They can only veto; they cannot make it pass.
9. **Not fragile.** λ = 0.5 must not flip the direction of the average change in CRPS on either synthetic set or on Berka. The Berka sensitivity group (2 or more a month) is informational only.
10. **My account can only veto,** with the shared thresholds.

**It stays off** if any condition fails, and a tie stays off. Anything unexpected (a persona that changes below the minimum history, a wider band, fewer than half of a counted archetype's origins moved) is written up and not tuned away.

**Prediction, written before any run.** It should help where the forecaster lost to the random walk in experiment 1: DRENT and ITIGHT in both synthetic sets. It could hurt SAL and TIGHT, whose scheduled income is already sharp; condition 2 would catch that. On Berka the zero-estimate origins are where it should help most; that stratum can only veto.

### 2026-10-17: Experiment 3: the minimum history changes from 20 to 8 unscheduled rows (the entry above stays as it was)
**What changes.** In the experiment 3 rule above, the trailing figure is used only with at least 91 days of history before the origin and at least **8** unscheduled rows in those 91 days (it said 20). The 91-day history requirement, the window, the trimming and everything else in that entry are unchanged.

**Why.** I counted before scoring anything (`docs/results/anchor_history_2026-10-17.md`): with 20 rows only 561 of the 8,000 Berka primary origins (7.0 percent) qualify, because the median account has about 9 unscheduled rows in 91 days. I chose 8 from those counts alone, as the highest minimum that keeps a majority of the origins (5,329, 66.6 percent; 10 would give 43.2 percent). Nothing had been scored. On the synthetic sets any minimum from 5 to 20 qualifies the same origins (700 of 800 in the 12-month set, the rest short of 91 days of history; 800 of 800 in the 24-month set), so the change does not touch them.

**Counted as a variant tried.** This change of the minimum is counted among the variants tried in the final report, next to λ = 1 and λ = 0.5.

**What it does not relax.** About a third of the Berka origins stay unchanged (the forecast is left as it is below the minimum). Condition 6 still averages over all origins, the unchanged ones included, so the 1 percent margin and the bootstrap bound are not relaxed.

**Added to the experiment 3 output.** The report gives the share of origins moved (those that meet the minimum and where the anchoring changed the forecast), for each synthetic set and for Berka, overall and within the zero-estimate stratum, with the number of accounts as well as origins. This is for reading the result; no condition depends on it except the existing "fewer than half of a counted archetype's origins moved" in condition 3.

### 2026-10-17: Experiment 3: how I read the measures in the rule, fixed before the run
These are readings of the words in the rule above, written down before the full run and not changed after seeing a result.
- **Condition 6 (Berka):** the 30-day CRPS is the CRPS of the balance on forecast day 30. The CRPS averaged over the 30 days is reported as information only and decides nothing.
- **Condition 5 (synthetic):** the CRPS skill against "balance stays" uses the mean over the 30 days, as in experiment 1.
- **Conditions 4 and 7:** the 80 percent coverage is read on both the 30-day coverage and the all-days coverage; either one breaking the 2 points fails the condition.
- **Which origins:** the dip measures (error, lowest-balance bias, coverage, Brier, dips caught) leave out origins already below the line (the buffer on the synthetic sets, "one month's outflow" on Berka). CRPS uses every origin.
- **Condition 10 (my account can only veto)** stays "not judged" until conditions 1 to 9 pass. Only then do I run the anchored forecast on my account.

### 2026-10-17: Experiment 3 (net-flow anchoring) is rejected and stays off
Report: `docs/results/anchor_2026-10-17.md` (synthetic and Berka development only; my account was not run). Judged one condition at a time with the readings I fixed before the run. **Verdict: it passes condition 1 and fails conditions 2 to 9 as judged; condition 10 is not judged**, because conditions 1 to 9 did not pass and my account is only run after they do. The forecaster's default is unchanged.

**Variants tried: 3** (λ = 1, λ = 0.5, and the minimum history of 8 rows instead of 20).

**Share of origins moved (λ = 1).** 12-month set: 700 of 800 (87.5 percent), in all 20 personas; the rest are short of 91 days of history. 24-month set: 800 of 800. Berka primary: 5,329 of 8,000 (66.6 percent), in 192 of 200 accounts; in the zero-estimate stratum 150 of 512 (29.3 percent), in 46 of its 83 accounts. λ = 0.5 moved the same origins within two.

**What failed.**
- **Condition 1 passed.** On both synthetic sets the origins it did not move equal arm 5, and the default equals arm 5 at 800 of 800 records. On Berka, 2,671 of the 8,000 origins are below the minimum (91 days of history or 8 unscheduled rows; here all 2,671 have enough days and too few rows, none is short of the days); of the 5,344 (origin, variant) pairs it did not move, 0 differ from the default. This check is now in the report (`unmoved_differences`) and has a test.
- **2 to 5 failed on both synthetic sets.** Archetypes worse by more than 1 percent: 5 of 8 on the 12-month set, 6 of 8 on the 24-month set; the 24-month averages over archetypes got worse (30-day error +6.1 percent, absolute bias +5.0 percent). IFAM's 30-day error went from $2,292 to $2,771 and its 30-day coverage from 54 to 32 percent. The CRPS and Brier skills against "balance stays" fell on the 24-month set (0.478 to 0.432 and 0.629 to 0.441).
- **6, 7 and 8 failed on Berka.** The 30-day CRPS changed by -0.2 percent (95 percent interval -0.8 to +0.3; it needed +1 percent with a lower end above 0). Dips caught at the 70 percent precision bar fell from 123 to 107 of 283 episodes. The zero-estimate stratum's 30-day error rose by 1.3 percent, over the 1 percent limit.
- **9 failed:** λ = 0.5 flips the direction on the 12-month set (both changes are about zero, so that flip says little) and on Berka.

**My prediction, written before the run, against the report (λ = 1; 30-day error and absolute lowest-balance bias, from the archetype tables).**
- *"It should help DRENT and ITIGHT in both synthetic sets."* Right in one place only: ITIGHT, 24-month, bias $4,455 to $4,060 (but its error rose $7,477 to $9,113). Wrong in the rest. DRENT, 12-month: error $938 to $938, bias $103 to $104 (no change). DRENT, 24-month: $919 to $991 and $107 to $129 (worse on both). ITIGHT, 12-month: error $7,735 to $7,853, bias $4,575 to $4,584 (worse error, flat bias).
- *"It could hurt SAL and TIGHT."* SAL was not hurt in either set (12-month $334 to $333 and $64 to $62; 24-month $227 to $226 and $7 to $5). TIGHT was not hurt on the 24-month set ($250 to $244, bias $-80 to $-80). On the 12-month set the report lists TIGHT as worse by more than 1 percent on the absolute bias ($65 to $66; error $321 to $322), so the prediction was met only technically, by the rule's own 1 percent measure, and the harm is negligible in dollars.
- *"On Berka the zero-estimate origins are where it should help most."* Partly. In that stratum the bias went $277 to $265 and the coverage moved towards 80 (30 days 44.2 to 45.5 percent), but the 30-day error rose $382 to $387 (+1.3 percent), against +0.2 percent over all Berka origins ($534 to $535), and the stratum failed its veto.
- *Not predicted:* the report lists as worse by more than 1 percent, on the 12-month set DHOME, IFAM, ITIGHT, SEM and TIGHT, and on the 24-month set DHOME, DRENT, FREE, IFAM, ITIGHT and SEM; and FREE, whose 30-day error fell in both sets ($1,338 to $1,269 and $617 to $553). On the 24-month set FREE is on the "worse" list only because of the absolute bias ($-59 to $-114), not the error, which fell.

**A lump observation, measured and not tested.** On the 24-month set the shift is large for IFAM and ITIGHT (median $20 and $58 a day) and tiny on the 12-month set ($0.4 and $1.6 a day). Those personas receive lumpy family transfers, and my reading is that the trailing figure counts them as ordinary unscheduled flow and carries them 30 days forward. I have not tested that.

**Nothing is tried to rescue it.** The window, the trimming, the minimum and λ stay as they were; I do not adjust the anchoring after seeing these results. Anything new would need its own adopt rule written first. The code stays in `eval/anchor.py` as an experiment and is not used by the forecaster.

### 2026-10-17: Experiment 4 (online combination with "balance stays"): the design and the adopt rule, fixed before anything is run
Not run yet. It follows the plan of 2026-10-17 and its common rules (rule zero, synthetic and Berka decide, my account can only veto, the shared thresholds, held-out data opened once at the end, every variant counted).

**The futures store.** The default forecaster is run once on the 20 development personas of each synthetic set and on the 200 Berka primary development accounts, and its futures are saved: for each origin 500 futures x 30 days of balance **in cents, as int32 (exact, nothing rounded)**, plus the balance at the origin, the buffer, the 30 realized balances, the origin date and two flags. One file per persona or account, written only when all 40 origins are done. Experiment 5 reuses it as its default arm. **Condition 1 is defined on it:** the default's p10, p50, p90, dip chance, median lowest balance and CRPS, recomputed from the saved paths, must equal the experiment 3 records exactly, at 800 of 800 origins on each synthetic set and 8,000 of 8,000 on Berka.

**What saving the futures costs, partly measured.** I saved one real Berka development account (40 origins, 500 futures each): 327 KB compressed, about 8 KB per origin, 2.3 MB raw; a synthetic persona took about 19 KB per origin. So 8,000 origins would be a rough 64 MB, **not a measured total** (it is one account). The time of the Berka pass is **not yet measured**: that account took 22 s (0.54 s per origin on one process) but was cheaper to simulate than average (experiment 3 averaged about 69 s per account per worker), so I plan on about 2 hours on 2 jobs.

**The combination (recentre).** With *s* the balance at the origin, *x_f(h)* future *f* on day *h* and *m(h)* the forecast's median that day, the combined future is *x_f(h) - (1 - w)(m(h) - s)* for a weight *w* (1 = today's forecast, 0 = "balance stays"). All futures slide by the same amount each day: the combined median is *w m(h) + (1 - w) s*, the **band width is unchanged**, and the band, each future's lowest balance and the dip chance are read from the combined futures (the dip chance is the share of combined futures that go under the buffer). It lowers the dip chance where the forecaster expects the balance to fall, and raises it where it expects money in. I did not choose *scaling* every future towards *s* (it narrows a band that already covers only 49 and 59 percent at 30 days) or *replacing a share* of the futures with the flat path (it puts a spike at *s* in the band and makes the dip chance exactly *w* times the old one). Neither is tried; either would need its own rule.

**Three variants** (a weight from the grid 0, 0.1, ..., 1; ties go to the larger weight): **(i)** fixed 50/50, w = 0.5 everywhere, nothing learned; **(ii)** one global weight; **(iii)** per-account weights. **Learning, past data only:** the loss is the mean over days 1 to 30 of the exact CRPS of the combined futures; at origin *t* only origins *t'* with *t' <= t - 35 days* count, so all 30 of their days were already posted. (iii): the w with the lowest average loss over the account's own resolved origins; with fewer than 8 the forecast is left unchanged (w = 1). (ii): the w with the lowest average, over the other accounts' resolved origins, of loss(w) divided by loss(1) (so large-dollar archetypes do not dominate); it needs at least 5 other accounts and 30 resolved origins, else w = 1. **The global weight is never fitted on what it is scored on:** synthetic, leave one *archetype* out (the three personas of an archetype come from one recipe, so leaving out one persona would leak), the 12- and 24-month sets never pooled; Berka, leave one account out; held-out data never used. Before any scoring I report, counts only, the share of origins that get a learned weight (for (iii), 27 of 40 per account by construction), and if fewer than half of the Berka origins do, I stop and propose a change first.

**It becomes the default only if a variant passes all of these** (readings fixed now; a tie stays off; the simplest passing variant wins, in the order (i), (ii), (iii); **(iii) comes last because it learns from heavily overlapping origins** and is chosen over the better of the others only if it beats it on condition 8 by at least 1 point with a bootstrap lower bound above 0):
1. **Unchanged where it should be.** Origins with w = 1 are identical to the default to the digit, and the saved futures reproduce the experiment 3 records exactly (above).
2. **No harm, each synthetic set.** No archetype is worse by more than 1 percent on the 30-day error or on the absolute lowest-balance bias; no persona by more than 5 percent.
3. **A real gain, each synthetic set.** An archetype is *net better* only if its 30-day error is better by more than 1 percent **and** its absolute bias is not worse by more than 1 percent; bias improving while the error gets worse can never count (the wording problem of experiment 3). At least 2 archetypes net better, each with at least half of its origins at w < 1; and the average over the 8 archetypes of the relative change must be below 0 for both the 30-day error and the absolute bias.
4. **Honest band.** No archetype ends further from 80 percent coverage than the default's by more than 2 points, read on both the 30-day and the all-days coverage; the 30-day band is not wider by more than 10 percent.
5. **CRPS not just flatter.** The mean CRPS skill against "balance stays" over the archetypes (mean over the 30 days) must not fall.
6. **Dips caught must not fall.** At the 70 percent precision bar (each method's own threshold, chosen by the same rule), dips caught on ahead must be no lower than the default's, on each synthetic set and on Berka. **"No lower" is deliberately strict**: a loss of one dip counts. The number of shortfall episodes is reported beside it (Berka has 283, so it decides).
7. **Dip Brier skill must not fall.** Synthetic: the mean Brier skill against "balance stays" over the archetypes with 5 or more dips must not fall (**deliberately strict**, the number of such archetypes and dips reported beside it). Berka: the Brier score of the dip chance ("fell below one month's outflow") is not worse than the default's by more than 1 percent.
8. **Berka margin.** The mean day-30 CRPS, accounts weighted equally, falls by at least 1 percent and the lower end of a 95 percent bootstrap interval is above 0 (accounts resampled, seed 2026, 2,000 draws).
9. **Berka other measures.** The 30-day error is not worse by more than 1 percent; the coverage (30-day and all-days) within 2 points.
10. **Vetoes**, with accounts as well as origins: the zero-estimate stratum within the shared thresholds, and the direction of the CRPS gain holding without the 16 mostly-cash accounts.
11. **Synthetic and Berka agree.** The mean CRPS falls on the 12-month set, on the 24-month set and on Berka.
12. **My account can only veto.** Run only if 1 to 11 pass.
Other readings: dip measures leave out origins already below the line; CRPS uses every origin; "moved" means w < 1 and the combined median differs on at least one day.

**Prediction, written before any run.** In experiment 1 the forecaster's CRPS skill against "balance stays" (n origins, personas) was, 12-month: DHOME +32.0 percent (120, 3), DRENT +16.4 (102, 3), FREE +19.8 (40, 1), IFAM +24.4 (120, 3), ITIGHT +3.0 (86, 3), SAL +86.3 (120, 3), SEM +70.0 (40, 1), TIGHT +74.8 (98, 3); 24-month: DHOME +37.9 (120, 3), DRENT +21.8 (120, 3), FREE +35.7 (30, 1), IFAM +31.9 (120, 3), ITIGHT +4.3 (81, 3), SAL +90.8 (120, 3), SEM +78.9 (40, 1), TIGHT +81.4 (86, 3). So I expect the learned weights to stay near 1 for SAL, SEM and TIGHT (almost no change), to fall furthest for ITIGHT, then DRENT and FREE, and DHOME and IFAM in between. I expect the fixed 50/50 to fail condition 2 (it hurts the archetypes where the skill is highest); the global weight to land near 1 and be close to a no-op, failing the gain conditions; per-account weights to give small CRPS gains in ITIGHT, DRENT and FREE and then to miss either condition 3 or the 1 percent Berka margin. I think the dips-caught condition (6) is the one most at risk. Most likely outcome: no variant is adopted. The 2026-10-12 blend (a different combination) also changed little.

**Order.** The rule is committed first; then the futures store is saved (on 2 jobs, after I close other apps); then the counts-only check of eligible origins; then a `--quick` run; the full run only after I say go.
