Now: such a series exists among the series the forecast places, so a salary that (b) judges possibly ended no longer counts. The futures, the point forecast, alerts and safe-to-spend do not read it; only the Forecast page, the Time Machine's saved steps (not shown on its page), the backtest and Berka records and the diagnostic records of `eval/diagnose.py` do.
- **The Berka report row** "origins whose forecast has an income series" is renamed "origins whose forecast places an income series", for future runs only. The figures already in `docs/results/berka_sanity_2026-10-17.md` (7,841 and 7,920 of 8,000) were counted with the old meaning and keep the old label. Backtest records from before today hold the old meaning in their `has_income` column. The committed Time Machine snapshots (regenerated 2026-11-10) also hold the old flag; they are not remade for it, as the original entry said.
- **Checked: no forecast number changed.** On the 20 synthetic development personas of the 12-month set (no held-out persona; the ids pass `assert_development_only`), at the 40 recorded weekly backtest origins (800 origins, 500 futures each), I built the default forecast with the code before the port (4314cd6) and after it (e9e9310, whose forecaster code is identical to this commit's), same origins and seed. Identical at 800 of 800 origins: every future's daily balances, the band and the other simulation numbers, the dip chance with and without irregular deposits, the point forecast, the placed items, the alert (194 of 800 origins alert) with its evidence, and safe-to-spend (717 of 800 origins have a safe amount). The intended change did show: `has_income` went from yes to no at 4 of 800 origins (2 personas), and 41 of 800 origins list a series as not placed. The check is committed as `eval/forecast_identity.py` (how to re-run it is in its docstring).
- **Time Machine snapshots: not regenerated.** The Forecast page never replays a snapshot; in demo mode it builds its forecast live from the demo database with the current code, so the new lines are always the new code's. The Time Machine page reads only the saved bands and alerts, which the check above shows are unchanged by the port; the old `has_income` flag in the saved steps is not displayed anywhere.
- **Berka reports go to a new dated file (my decision, 2026-11-17).** A sanity run used to merge its block into `docs/results/berka_sanity_2026-10-17.md`. Now each run writes `docs/results/berka_sanity_<date of the run>.md`; a second group the same day joins that day's file, and an older dated file is never changed (a test checks it).

## Going public

### 2026-11-17: I go public before the forecaster's held-out test
- **Decision.** I make the repository public before running the forecaster's held-out final test. The forecaster is not final yet (experiments 5 and 6 are not started).
- **What the README says because of this.** Every forecaster number is on development data only: the synthetic development personas and the development accounts of the public Berka bank dataset. The held-out test has not been run. The README leads with the categorizer and the evaluation method, not the forecaster.
- **The held-out data stays unopened.** The held-out personas and Berka held-out accounts are not opened by going public; the test runs once, later, when the forecaster is final, and nothing is tuned after it. Going public changes nothing about that rule.
- **No deployment for now.** Demo mode stays in the code, but I am not putting the app on Streamlit Community Cloud yet.

### 2026-11-17: What from my real data may be public
Categorizer and merchant-matching accuracy on my labelled rows may be public, as percentages with n (no amounts, merchants or dates); the two such numbers already here stay. Never public: money-revealing metrics, meaning anything the forecaster scores on my account (dollar errors, dips caught, coverage, Brier), balances, amounts and income timing.

### 2026-11-17: Five local brand names in the generator renamed to made-up ones
- **Decision.** I renamed the five small local brands the synthetic generator used (two cafés, two restaurants and an online shop) to made-up names of the same length, and the AI company used as a subscription in one test to a made-up one. Concordia Bookstore and uBreakiFix stay: my school is on my CV, and uBreakiFix is a public chain. The generator's store numbers were already random.
- **What it changed (checked with `eval/forecast_identity.py`, 20 synthetic development personas, 800 origins).** The generated amounts and dates are identical; only the merchant text changed. 794 of 800 origins are identical. The other 6 (3 origins each of v2-DRENT-03 and v2-TIGHT-02) changed slightly, because those personas' regular café and restaurant purchases form card series whose key holds the name, and each future's random draws are keyed by it. One dip chance moved from 41.8 to 42.8 percent; no alert switched on or off; the point forecast and safe-to-spend are identical everywhere. The demo Time Machine steps are identical (21 of 21 files); only the 7 snapshot databases were regenerated, since they hold the names. Two tests pin the generator byte for byte, and their values were retaken: all 20 development exports and answer keys differ from the old ones by the renamed names only (40 of 40 files identical after the name mapping), and of the 9 pinned forecasts with the recurring fix switched off (not the default), 1 changed (v2-SAL-01, 2026-01-05), the same mechanism.
- **Categorizer.** 144 of the 661 synthetic training rows have new text, with the same categories, so a categorizer retrained after this change differs slightly. The categorizer holdout result stays as recorded and is not rescored.
- **Merchant resolution.** `eval/resolution_variants.py` splits the merchants into a development and a test half by sorting their names, then a seeded shuffle. A renamed brand sorts to a different place, so a rerun would put different merchants (including one former development merchant) in the test half, and the variant draws change too. So the merchant-resolution test half cannot be rescored: its recorded result stays as recorded, and any new resolution score needs a new test set.
- **Recorded results stay as recorded.** Before the final held-out test I rerun the baselines once on the renamed code, so the final comparison is like for like.

### 2026-11-17: The history scrub goes to a new repository, not a force-push
- **Decision.** I do not force-push a rewritten history over the existing GitHub repository: GitHub keeps commits that a force-push rewrote away reachable by their id, so the old text would stay readable. Instead I rewrite the history on a fresh clone and push it to a new, empty GitHub repository, created only after I approve it. The old repository is renamed `lookahead-archive` and stays private.
- **Lost:** a force-push over the existing repository, with a request to GitHub to purge the cached commits. It depends on GitHub support and leaves the old commits readable until they act.
- **Email.** Every commit, old and new, uses one author and committer address: my gmail address, which is already public and which I keep. The rewrite changes the old yahoo address on every commit to it. No noreply address. The scrub report names the public address and flags any other one.
- **What the rewrite removes or changes** comes from the scrub scan (`tools/scrub_scan.py`): at least the old scrub report, which quotes amounts, from every commit; then what I decide from its keep, reword or remove list.

### 2026-11-17: The pattern builder asks for --mine; the extra whole-file removals live in my private file
- **Decision.** A bare run of `python -m tools.scrub_patterns` reads nothing and stops with a message: `--mine` is required. With it, the paths I leave out default to my private folder; a default file that does not exist is skipped by name, and a path I type that does not exist stops the run (a typo must not quietly drop patterns).
- **Decision.** The public scan code names only one whole file that leaves the history: the old scrub report. Every other whole file to remove is a `leave:` line (one repository path) of my private extras file, so no planning or local file is named in public code.
- **Lost:** defaults that read my private files on a bare run (one mistyped command reads my bank files), and the removal list written in the scan's code.

### 2026-11-18: How the history rewrite is done and checked
- **Tool.** `git-filter-repo` (a git tool installed with Homebrew, not a project dependency), on a fresh clone. It removes the whole files that leave the history, replaces text in every version of every file (one rule for every old version of each line I reworded, so earlier commits get the new wording too, not just the tip), moves the old yahoo address to my gmail address, and rewords the two commit messages of the scrub tool that say "AI tool".
- **What the new repository gets:** `main`, and the tags `exp4-final` and `proposals-v2-final` (where the code of experiment 4 and of proposals part (b) lives; they go through the same rewrite and checks). Not `demo`, `archive/lightgbm-prototype`, `runs-2026-10-16` or any other old branch.
- **Commit ids.** The rewrite gives every commit a new id. After it, one commit replaces the old ids cited in DECISIONS, the README and `docs/` at the tip with the new ones, from filter-repo's commit map, plus one line saying that ids inside older commits refer to the history before the rewrite. No mapping table.
- **Checks on the new clone:** every old wording is found 0 times in every commit; the scrub scan with the old wordings as my `phrase:` patterns finds 0 paths; the tip's files equal today's tip except the files that leave; the full suite passes.
- **Lost:** fixing the wording at the tip only (older commits would still show it); a mapping table of old and new ids.
- **Done 2026-11-18.** The commit ids cited in DECISIONS and `docs/` at the tip are the rewritten history's ids; ids inside older commits, and ids of commits that are not in this repository, refer to the history before the rewrite.
- **Persona C and the old brand names in older commits.** I chose to give every older commit today's versions of C's export, answer key and Time Machine snapshot, today's C settings in the generator and its tests, and today's made-up brand names (older versions of the demo snapshot databases become today's), so commits before the rewrite may not reproduce or run Persona C as it was, and their pinned export hashes may not match. Lost: leaving C and the old names as they were in older commits (they tied the generator to my account).

### 2026-11-18: Persona C's settings replaced with made-up values
- **Decision.** Every setting of the demo persona C is now made up: the opening balance, rent, bill and subscription, tuition amounts and dates, the family memos before each term, the top-up rule (threshold, delays, amounts, rounding, gaps), the posting-day rule, the quiet month, the spending profile and its merchants and weights, and the returned e-Transfer. The story stays: a student funded by family credit memos, with tuition each term. The C tests now check C against its own made-up profile.
- **What it changes.** Only C's synthetic export and answer key and its Time Machine snapshot (`synthetic-C`). A and B are byte-identical, their snapshots too. The 30 mix-v2 personas use none of C's code; the forecast identity check on the 20 development personas is identical at 800 of 800 origins (every field). Results recorded before this stay as recorded (the forecaster's results use mix-v2 personas only).
- **Categorizer.** Its synthetic training rows come from A, B and C, so the next `python -m lookahead.categorize` run trains a slightly different model. The recorded categorizer results stay as recorded and are not rescored.
- **Known, not fixed:** the planted identical pair can be picked on the day after the export date and drop out of the export (the double charge cannot: its pick leaves the last day out). One of C's made-up café weights was set so its pair stays inside the window; fixing the pick would change the mix-v2 personas, so it waits.
- **Bug fixed on the way:** the Time Machine's evidence lines read a big event's group name, which a saved step never has (`saved_evidence` leaves it out), so a saved step with a big event stopped the page. It now says "big event" (test). The Forecast page reads stored alert episodes, which always have the name.

## Categorizer v2

### 2026-11-18: Categorizer v2 groundwork, all off by default
- **Built.** A merchant-text cleaner, a public directory of 267 big Canadian chains (national and Quebec, all unverified), a RAG categorizer for new merchants (Haiku 4.5 with my labelled examples and directory entries, strict JSON answers with citations), one setup question for it, and an evaluation harness. Spec: `docs/specs/2026-11-18-categorizer-v2.md` (DRAFT).
- **Off by default.** Each piece has its own setting; none is on until I turn it on, and RAG only after I answer yes to the question. With everything off, the categorizer's outputs on personas A, B and C are identical to a snapshot taken on main before any v2 code (`tests/test_categorizer_v2_identity.py`).
- **Directory similarity threshold:** 0.60, from the synthetic merchants that are not chains only (one 0.05 step above the best score any of them reaches, plus one step of margin). Similarity matching has its own switch, also off.
- **Nothing scored.** No accuracy on any data, real or synthetic. The harness ran only as its synthetic dry run, which prints counts. No API call was made.
- **Adopt rule:** still a draft (`docs/specs/2026-11-18-categorizer-v2-adopt-rule-DRAFT.md`). The harness refuses to score until a final version is committed and unchanged.
- **Lost:** a hidden toggle for RAG (one question instead).
- **Fixed before merging (reviewer).** A blind row I label in the app would have reached the RAG examples while the harness scores (c), inflating its gain over (b). The harness now refuses to score when any blind row carries a label saved in the app, and the RAG examples leave the blind set out. Tests on persona C.
- **Merged into main (2026-11-18)** as a fast-forward to b286f6f, after the full suite (2,657 passed), ruff, the identity test on A, B and C (identical with everything off) and the reviewer's PASS.
- **My answers to the groundwork's questions (2026-11-18).** The directory's categories stay as picked, and the other points stay as built. The adopt-rule file name is approved (`docs/specs/2026-11-18-categorizer-v2-adopt-rule.md` once final). The rule's numbers (the gains for (b) and (c), the bootstrap bound, the known-merchant guard) are decided with my advisor before labelling; the draft stays as it is until then. Directory similarity stays off and is not a separate variant: (b) is the cleaner plus exact and alias lookups. Before the cleaner is turned on, my next real-data check compares the cleaner with my stored preferences.

### 2026-11-18: Categorizer v2 harness and directory fixes before anything is scored; blind labelling mode (spec)
- **Scored once, by the blind selection.** A scoring run is keyed on the blind set's row selection: the sorted fingerprints of every row in the labels file, labelled or not. A run is refused when any of its rows was in an earlier scored run, so editing a label (or adding and removing rows) never allows a second scoring; `--again` still overrides and is recorded. Every run goes in the ledger, refused ones too, with the reason.
- **One ledger.** Every run uses the one ledger in data/private/; there is no option to point at another, so a fresh file cannot reset "scored once".
- **One adopt-rule file.** The harness accepts only `docs/specs/2026-11-18-categorizer-v2-adopt-rule.md`, committed and unchanged since its commit; any other path is refused.
- **Short directory keys match only exactly.** A chain name or alias of 5 letters or fewer (after cleaning) matches only an equal key: never by similarity, never by the "?" wildcard. Tests with Gap, Bell, Shell and Cora against made-up local names. Side effect, to know before the RAG prompt is frozen: short chains (IGA, SAQ, STM, Maxi, Metro, Uber and others) are no longer offered to the RAG prompt as similar directory entries, only through an exact or alias match or a longer alias. Exact matches of a short name to an unrelated local merchant are still possible: my real-data check of the directory comes before it is turned on.
- **No key, no Send button.** Without an API key the send buttons are hidden and the "what is needed" text shows instead.
- **Blind labelling mode (spec).** `python -m eval.categorizer_v2 label --db PATH --selection PATH --labels PATH`, in the terminal. The first run freezes the selection: it writes the labels file (inside data/private/) with every selected row and an empty category, and refuses if the file already exists with another set of rows. Then it shows one row at a time: the bank's merchant text only, never a predicted category, confidence or source (it computes none), and the 17 categories by number; I type a number, `s` to skip or `q` to stop. Each answer is saved at once; a later run continues where I stopped. Tests on synthetic rows: the frozen selection, the refusal, no prediction in the output, resume.
- **Lost:** a labelling page in the app (the page would sit next to the model's guesses; the terminal shows nothing but the text).
- **Merged into main (2026-11-18)** as a fast-forward to 0505fab, after the full suite (2,692 passed) and the reviewer's PASS on both commits. Open: the ledger path is relative, so a run from a worktree would use that worktree's own (empty) ledger; run the harness from the main folder only. Fixed the same day: the ledger is an absolute path in the main folder's data/private (found through git's shared folder), the same file from any folder; test.

### 2026-11-18: Categorizer v2 adopt rule: my decisions, and the harness changed to match (before anything is scored)
The draft's open points are settled (my decisions with my advisor's input); the rule text is `docs/specs/2026-11-18-categorizer-v2-adopt-rule-DRAFT.md` until I approve it as the final file. Nothing is drawn, labelled or scored.
- **Two blind groups, drawn once at random with seed 0, frozen before labelling:** A, new merchants (up to 150 rows from 2026-10-01, at most 3 per merchant, no training labels); K, known merchants (60 unlabelled rows of merchants with training labels), for the guard. Shuffled together, so the labelling order does not tell the group. The `candidates` command now draws both groups and never overwrites an existing selection; the labels file carries each row's group (never shown while labelling).
- **Minimum:** fewer than 100 rows for A: nothing is drawn, and scoring is refused. My readings: "qualify" is counted after the cap of 3; at scoring, the count is labelled, scorable A rows (skipped rows do not count).
- **(b) uses exact and alias directory matches only**; similarity matching is not part of this test (the code sets it off explicitly).
- **(c) is compared with (b), not with (a)**, same thresholds (at least 3 points and a merchant-level bootstrap 2.5th percentile above 0). The harness used to compare every variant with (a); it now computes (b) vs (a) and (c) vs (b), each with the guard, and writes "passes" to the ledger.
- **Guard:** on K, (b) and (c) may each get at most 1 more row wrong than (a), counted in rows.
- **Also refused:** a group A row whose merchant has training labels by the time of scoring.
- **Lost:** the draft's single new-merchant set with known-merchant rows "in the same file" (the old `candidates` command picked new merchants only, so the guard would have had almost no rows); the 5th percentile (one-sided) bound; testing directory similarity in (b).
- **Readings kept from the draft, to confirm in the final text:** A merchants must also be first seen on or after 2026-10-01; K has no per-merchant cap.
- **What it can detect (made-up numbers; `python -m eval.categorizer_v2_power`):** 150 rows in 75 merchants, 200 trials each, the rule's bootstrap (2,000 draws). When (b) breaks no row that (a) got right, a true gain of 5 points passes 83% of the time and 8 points 96%. When it also breaks 5% of those rows, 8 points passes 55% and 10 points 76%. So the rule's line "only roughly 8 to 10 points is likely to pass" holds when a change also breaks some rows.
- **After the reviewer (PASS, non-blocking notes):** the draw and the labelling refuse until the final rule is committed; the start day is fixed at 2026-10-01 (no `--since`); the draw is written to the ledger and a second draw is refused whatever the output path; tests check that labelled and old-holdout rows never reach the written selection, and that the labelling prompt is the bank text only.
- Tests: the cap, the 150 and 60 rows, the seed, the mixed order, the minimum at selection and at scoring, no redraw, the rule before the draw and the labelling, (b) without similarity, (c) against (b), the 3-point and bootstrap conditions, the guard, a group A merchant that gained labels.

### 2026-11-18: Categorizer v2 adopt rule: my four answers, and the rule put in force
- **(c) only if (b) passes too:** (c) contains (b), so adopting it would bring in parts that failed. In code: (c)'s comparison carries `base_passes`.
- **Guard minimum:** scoring is refused with fewer than 40 labelled group K rows (skipped rows do not count). Lost: no minimum, reporting the K count only.
- **No cap per merchant in K:** K looks like real use. Lost: at most 3 per merchant, as in A.
- **Rows I skipped in the label review are left out of the draw,** with every row that has a current decision of mine (category or skip). Lost: allowing them (they are rows I already found hard, which would skew the test).
- **Wording:** the "what this test can detect" line now gives the power check's numbers (5 points: about 83% if (b) breaks nothing; 8 to 10 points, 55% / 76%, if it breaks about 5% of the rows (a) got right). The 0.60 line stays.
- The rule is committed as `docs/specs/2026-11-18-categorizer-v2-adopt-rule.md` in its own commit; the DRAFT file is removed (its history stays in git). A test checks that the code's numbers are the rule's numbers. Nothing is drawn, labelled or scored yet.
- **After the second review (PASS), safety only; the rule text is unchanged:** `candidates --count` prints how many rows could go in A (after the cap) and K, and draws, writes and logs nothing, because a draw with too few K rows would use up the one-time draw and could never be scored. Scoring refuses if the rule's text differs from the one recorded with the draw. A database error while reading my label-review decisions now stops the draw instead of counting as "no decisions". Kept as the reviewer noted, not changed: the 95% line warns and still prints the comparisons (stopping to look is my step); "the same session" is my wording for one shuffled pass.
- **After the third review (PASS), safety only; the rule text is unchanged:** scoring is refused without a draw in the ledger, and when the labels file does not hold exactly the drawn rows (a hand-made labels file cannot be scored). The old-holdout lookup no longer takes a database error for "no holdout" (only a missing table). A test goes through the whole path on a synthetic copy: count, draw, freeze, label from the answer key, score.
- **Merged into main (2026-11-18)** as a fast-forward to d44efcf, after the full suite (2,721 passed) and the reviewer's PASS on every commit. The rule's commit (dfb1fc6) is unchanged by the merge. Nothing drawn, labelled or scored.

### 2026-11-18: Categorizer v2: the deciding set moves to an external public set; my rows become a veto (adopt rule version 2)
- **Why:** version 1 (dfb1fc6) needed at least 100 new-merchant rows of mine. My count (`candidates --count`, nothing drawn) gave 18 rows from 16 new merchants since 2026-10-01, and 76 known-merchant rows. At that pace, 100 would take months. My account is too small to decide v2, and by my rules my real data should only veto. Version 1 was never used: nothing drawn, labelled or scored.
- **Searched for real public data first:** no public set of real, labelled bank descriptions is usable. A 259k "real" set has an undocumented origin and holds customers' names; a 4.5M set mixes unnamed real sources with synthetic rows (gated, 10 coarse categories); MoneyData (CC BY 4.0, about 6,500 real rows) has bank transaction types, not spending categories. Nothing Canadian or French.
- **Lost: synthetic new merchants from my own generator.** It would grade itself: my generator has 28 merchants, 22 of them are directory chains, and the directory agrees with the generator's label on all 485 of those rows (625 synthetic training rows). The directory's 0.60 threshold was also chosen on the generator's other merchants.
- **Won: DoDataThings v2** (synthetic, US, MIT): an independent author, with no merchant chosen with my code in mind. It allows a 600-row deciding sample, so a true gain of about 4 points is detectable instead of 8 to 10 with version 1's 150 rows (power check: 150 rows in 75 merchants, 200 trials). Everything in the deciding run is public: split by merchant (40% to test, seed 0), (a), (b) and (c) trained, tuned and given RAG examples from the train part only; my labels, my settings and my generator's rows are not used.
- **Found while checking the download** (counts only; the deciding sample has not been scored):
  - The file has no merchant column and no amounts. The merchant key is rebuilt from the generator's own name list, read as text (5 frozen steps; squashed, glued and truncated spellings included after a first version put one chain in both parts).
  - The only public version of that list is dated after the dataset; 10 of its 1,008 names never occur.
  - A dry run of (a) on held-back train merchants (not the deciding sample) gave 68.8% (n = 475 rows, 201 merchants), on an earlier grouping, so there is room for a gain.
  - The Canadian directory and a cleaner that keeps US city names will answer fewer rows here than on Québec data. Not tuned.
- **Dropped categories:** Transfer, Income and Fees (my bank rules decide them in the app, not the model), Insurance and Travel (no category of mine). Food delivery and Restaurants count as the same when scoring.
- **The veto on my rows:**
  - It runs what I would switch on in the app: the deployed model, then the cleaner and directory, then RAG with my labelled merchants.
  - Thresholds: at most 2 rows net worse on new-merchant rows, at most 1 more wrong on known-merchant rows.
  - Minimums: 15 new-merchant rows to draw and score, 40 labelled known-merchant rows to score.
  - My rows are drawn last, after the public run is scored, so newer months count.
  - Adopted = passes on the public set AND no veto.
- **Lost:** a merchant-name grouping without the generator list (less exact, and more shared keys between the two parts); keeping Transfer, Income and Fees (that would grade the model on rows it never sees in the app).
- **After the reviewer's FAIL (two blocking points), before the rule is committed:**
  - The first scored public run under this exact rule text decides. A later `--again` run is recorded but decides nothing, and a run under another rule text counts for nothing; the draw gate and the "adopted" line use only those entries.
  - The harness refuses a rule without the version 2 heading, so version 1 cannot come back by a revert.
  - A category missing from the mapping stops the run; the RAG cache path is absolute.
  - 2-letter chain names (BP) count as merchants: before this, BP fell back to keys like "BP HIK" and sat in both parts.
  - `summary` prints how many cleaned names the train part and the sample share: 0.
  - The final grouping: 1,072 merchants; train 28,656 rows / 643 merchants; test 19,344 / 429; sample 600 / 356.
  - Disclosed in the rule: my dry run of (a) (68.8%, n = 475 rows, 201 merchants) touched merchants that are now test merchants: 58 of them, 44 in the sample, 10 of its rows are sample rows. Only (a) was run; nothing was tuned from it.
- **My answers to the three open points (2026-11-18):**
  - The one public scoring is `score --rag --send`, all three variants in one run. Anything else is refused, so (c) cannot be added later or run with an empty cache.
  - (c) is adopted only if (b) is adopted (`adoptions`), because (c) contains (b).
  - The deciding sample stays as drawn. The dry run touched it only through (a)'s accuracy, which cannot favour (b) over (a); it is disclosed in the rule.
  - Lost: scoring (a) and (b) alone; judging (c) on its own; redrawing from untouched merchants. A different seed after looking would itself be a choice made after looking.
- **Merged into main (2026-11-18)** as a fast-forward, after the full suite (2,755 passed) and the reviewer's PASS on every commit. The rule's version 2 commit (8254126) comes before any public scoring or draw. Nothing scored, drawn or labelled yet.

### 2026-11-19: Categorizer v2, public result: nothing adopted; RAG not measured (an answer-parser bug found after scoring, now fixed)
- **The result stands** (the one public scoring, `score --rag --send`, 2026-11-18, under rule version 2, 8254126; n = 600 rows from 356 test merchants, trained on 28,656 rows from 643 train merchants): (a) current 75.2%; (b) cleaner+directory 74.3% (16 rows from the directory, 584 from the model); (c) +RAG 74.3%, with exactly the same sources as (b). (b) vs (a): -0.8 points (446 against 451 right of 600; 75.2 and 74.3 are rounded), bootstrap 2.5th percentile -3.8: fails. (c) vs (b): +0.0: fails, and (b) failed anyway. Nothing is adopted, v2 stays off. No `--again`. No real draw: there is nothing to veto, so the one-time draw stays unused. No variant was above 95%.
- **Why the cleaner lost** (a diagnostic run afterwards, not a scoring: (a) and (b) retrained on the same public data, no RAG, no ledger, no private file; it reproduced 75.2% and 74.3%). (b) fixed 26 rows (a) got wrong and broke 31 it got right, from 23 and 25 merchants; 420 right in both, 123 wrong in both.
  - **Retuning noise:** 417 of the 600 rows were not changed by the cleaner, yet 19 were fixed and 15 broken there. (b) trains on cleaned names, so its tuning picks other settings (text pieces of 2 to 5 characters instead of 3 to 5; unsure below 98% confidence instead of 88%), and rows like APPLE TV+ or GRAMMARLY flip (6 of those 15 breaks go to Shopping).
  - **Stripped clues:** on the 183 rows whose name the cleaner changed, 7 fixed and 16 broken. Payment wrappers and store numbers carried the category: "TST*" (a restaurant payment system) means a restaurant here (221 of 221 train-part rows that carry it), and "TST* BLUE BOTTLE #118" cleaned to "BLUE BOTTLE" went to Shopping. These test merchants are never in training, so the model leans on exactly those patterns.
  - **The directory:** 15 of 16 right (n = 16), against 12 for (a) on the same rows; 4 fixed, 1 broken (7-ELEVEN: Groceries in my directory, Shopping in this set, a category-definition difference). It reaches few rows on a US set, as the rule expected.
- **Why RAG answered nothing** (found after scoring): RAG was asked about 347 flagged rows (332 distinct names, so 332 Haiku calls). My counts on the private cache: 332 of 332 answers were wrapped in a code fence (```json ... ```) and refused as "not JSON" by the strict parser, so none was taken and each row kept the model's category; 0 empty; 7 of 332 also stopped at the 100-token limit. The parser had only ever met a test client answering bare JSON, and one test even required a fenced answer to be refused. So the public (c) number measures a broken parser, not RAG.
- **Fixed on `rag-fence-fix`, after scoring, without rescoring:**
  - One code fence around the whole answer is removed before parsing; text before or after a fence is still refused.
  - The token limit goes from 100 to 300: the longest valid answer, fenced, fits even at one token per character (tested).
  - A loud warning, and `rag_asked` / `rag_answered` / `rag_warning` in the ledger, when RAG is asked about rows and answers none.
  - The app counts "unsure" apart from answers it cannot use.
  - The prompt is unchanged. Saved answers keep their old verdict (the cache stores the decision), so the public cache would still reproduce the 0.
  - Not known: whether the 7 cut-off answers were long JSON or text after it. A rule for RAG must stop when too many answers are refused.
- **Lost:** rescoring with `--again` (it would decide nothing under the rule and use up the sample); treating "RAG +0.0" as a RAG result.
- **Next:** a v3 adopt rule testing RAG alone ((a)+RAG vs (a)) on a new split, drafted, not committed.

### 2026-11-19: RAG alone, adopt rule version 3 committed before its harness
- **The rule** (`docs/specs/2026-11-19-categorizer-rag-adopt-rule-v3.md`, committed alone as 1654555, before any code for it): (a)+RAG against (a), on a new split of the same public set. Version 2's 356 deciding-sample merchants go to training; of the other 716, 286 are test merchants (seed 1). The sample is 600 rows from 275 merchants. The test is the same as version 2's: at least 3 points AND the bootstrap's 2.5th percentile above 0.
- **My prediction, written before scoring:** (a)+RAG about 80–88% against (a).
- **The void rule:** a run where RAG gives no category, or where more than 5% of its answers (one per distinct request) are refused as not usable, is void. It stops before any accuracy, is logged with its counts by reason, and its cache is moved aside, so the next run asks every answer again. Version 2's zero would have been void under this rule.
- **Lost:** reusing version 2's 600 rows (already scored, and I had studied (a)'s errors on them); fresh rows of the same test merchants (the same merchants, 1,436 rows sharing a cleaned name with the 600); a split drawn with v2's permutation (the counts I looked at came from `choice`, so the rule pins `choice`).
- **Harness** (`eval/categorizer_rag_v3.py`): `split` prints counts only. `score --rag --send` is the one run, with its own cache (`rag_v3.json`) and its own ledger name. Scored once, and the first valid run under this exact rule text decides. `adopt_rule_status` now takes the version's heading; each harness also accepts only its own rule file by path (the v2 heading occurs in the v3 text, so for v2 the path is what keeps them apart). The split command gives the rule's counts: train 36,993 rows / 786 merchants, test 11,007 / 286, sample 600 / 275, 0 merchants shared, 0 cleaned names shared with version 2's sample.
- **After the reviewer's PASS, safety only; the rule text is unchanged:**
  - The one draw of my rows (`candidates`) was still open under version 2's gate. It now also waits until RAG passes version 3's public run, because that draw is version 3's veto.
  - A crashed run is recorded (`crashed`, with the error type only).
  - A run with uncommitted changes to tracked files is refused, and every entry records the commit the code ran from.
  - An `--again` run never decides.
  - The shared-names count uses the cleaner's match form (still 0).
  - Not changed: the 95% line prints and does not stop the run (stopping to look is my step, as in version 2).
- **After the reviewer's FAIL (one blocking point), safety only; the rule text is unchanged:**
  - `--again` is refused before a first scoring. Otherwise a first run with `--again` would decide nothing and block every later run.
  - "Already scored" means a comparison was recorded, so a run that crashed before it decided nothing.
  - Ctrl-C and errors while the data loads are recorded as crashed.
  - Refusals record the code commit too, and git that cannot tell counts as uncommitted.
  - A test shows the draw opens on a v3 pass and stays shut on a fail.
- **After the second FAIL (one blocking point), safety only; the rule text is unchanged:**
  - The comparison is now recorded before any accuracy is printed. Before this, a crash between the printed accuracies and the recorded comparison would have shown the result without spending the scoring.
  - "Already scored" now means any recorded v3 comparison, whatever the sample or rule text. Changing either after scoring therefore cannot open a second deciding run: the verdict stays the first one, or none.

### 2026-11-19: RAG v3's one run was void; adopt rule version 4 (two parser changes)
- **The void run** (version 3, 1654555; the void rule worked as designed). It stopped before any accuracy, and no label was looked at.
  - RAG was asked about 323 rows and gave a category for 257.
  - Of 302 answers, 244 were usable and 58 refused (19%, the limit is 5%): 45 "no citation" and 13 "not JSON".
  - Its cache was moved aside, as the rule says.
- **Diagnosis** (my counts on the moved-aside cache, plus 10 raw replies, 5 per reason; no labels: I chose not to run the label match, so the change was decided without knowing whether those answers were right):
  - All 45 "no citation" answers were a category with `"cited": []`: Haiku answering from its own knowledge.
  - All 13 "not JSON" answers were a fenced `{"category": null, ...}` followed by an explanation. One reply had two JSON objects ("Travel", then null).
  - The largest answer was 137 tokens, and 0 hit the limit.
- **Won: version 4** (`docs/specs/2026-11-19-categorizer-rag-adopt-rule-v4.md`, committed alone), with two parser changes only; everything else is as in version 3, prompt included.
  - (1) One JSON object with text around it, inside or outside a fence, is read from that object. More than one object is "unsure" if at least one has the answer shape (exactly "category" and "cited"); otherwise it is refused.
  - (2) A category with no citation is "unsure", and the row keeps (a)'s guess. RAG must cite its evidence; answering from Haiku's own knowledge is the separate LLM fallback.
  - My prediction stays: (a)+RAG about 80–88% on the deciding sample (n = 600 rows, 275 merchants).
- **Lost:**
  - Changing the prompt to forbid explanations (that changes what is measured).
  - Treating a no-citation category as RAG's answer (that measures Haiku's own knowledge, not retrieval).
  - Reading the first of several objects (in the one two-object reply, the first was a category it then took back).
  - Looking at whether the no-citation answers were right before choosing (choosing after seeing the result).
- **Code:**
  - The app's RAG uses the same parser.
  - A saved reply is now always read again with today's parser. This replaces the 2026-11-19 note that saved answers keep their old verdict. Version 2 is closed, so nothing changes there.
  - The harness is now `eval/categorizer_rag_v4.py`, with its own ledger name (`external-rag-v4`) and its own cache (`rag_v4.json`), which must start empty (an existing file is refused, except right after a crashed v4 run or for an `--again` run).
  - The one draw of my rows now waits for version 4's pass.

### 2026-11-19: RAG v4, public run: passes (adoption waits for the veto on my rows)
- **The result** (rule version 4, 96746b5; the one valid public run, after version 2's parser bug and version 3's void run). On the deciding sample, n = 600 rows from 275 test merchants, trained on 36,993 rows from 786 train merchants:
  - (a) 75.8% (455 right).
  - (a)+RAG 86.3% (518 right).
  - Gain +10.5 points, merchant-level bootstrap 2.5th percentile +7.0 (2,000 draws, 275 merchants resampled).
  - Passes on the public set. Nothing was above 95%.
- **RAG's answers:** asked about 323 rows, it gave a category for 258. Of 302 answers: 245 ok, 10 unsure, 1 unsure with more than one object, 46 unsure with no citation, 0 refused. The run was valid under the 5% void rule.
- **My prediction, written before scoring:** (a)+RAG about 80–88%. The result, 86.3%, is inside it.
- **Not adopted yet:** by the rule, RAG is offered in the app (still off by default) only if it is also not vetoed on my real rows: Part 2, the one draw, blind labelling, at most 2 rows net worse on new merchants and at most 1 on known ones.
- Synthetic, US, public: this says RAG helps the categorizer on an independent benchmark. It says nothing yet about my account.
